希布利·巴加拉格尽管很想在城中多逗留片刻、安享百姓的膜拜，但终究听从了船长的劝诱。于是他跟随船长登上一旁的小舟，水手们摇橹将二人送上了大船。登船之后，船长当即扬帆起航，不多时巨舰便驶入了茫茫深海之中。船上为希布利·巴加拉格辟出了一间专属的寝舱，船长则住在另一间。当希布利·巴加拉格步入寝舱时，赫然望见卧榻床头栖息着一只苍鹰；它的双眼红如红宝石，利喙弯曲宛若弯刀锋刃。他当即大声呼唤船长，船长闻声赶来；可一见到苍鹰，船长一把扯下头顶的缠头巾狠狠砸向苍鹰，随后张开双臂扑上前去试图将它擒获；苍鹰在舱房的角落之间盘旋飞舞，船长在后面穷追不舍。接着船长拔出一柄利剑，苍鹰却侧身掠过他，猛然落在他后脑勺上，利爪狠狠撕扯他的头皮毛发，利喙越过他的额头猛啄双眼。希布利·巴加拉格听见苍鹰尖声啼叫出那个名字：“卡拉兹（Karaz）”，他定睛细看船长的面容，方才认出此人正是神怪卡拉兹。他吓得浑身剧烈颤抖，痛骂自己的轻信盲从，深知自己已落入了神怪的魔掌，在这凶险万状的茫茫沧海之上，唯有这只苍鹰是他的唯一盟友。待苍鹰从神怪头上硬生生拔下一根特定的发丝之际，神怪浑身僵硬如铁，通体如熔炉中的赤铜般泛起刺目的火红；随后他沉重地砸穿了船底，直坠入下方的海渊之中，海水遇到他顿时如滚烫的铁条淬水般嘶嘶沸腾、冒起浓浓黑烟。希布利·巴加拉格急忙感谢先知，盛赞神鹰，但苍鹰却如箭般疾射出船舱，他紧随其后来到甲板上，看哪！整艘巨舰早已陷入熊熊火海，苍鹰被一圈烈焰死死围在中央；火舌随着它腾空而起，封死了所有去路。正当希布利·巴加拉格注视着苍鹰之际，烈焰向他扑面卷来，引燃了他的衣袍。他不敢迟疑，急忙将灵魂托付于大慈大悲之主，诵念信仰誓词，一头扎入了茫茫波涛之中。

===Original===

When he rose, the ship had vanished, and all was darkness where it had been; so he buffeted with the billows, thinking his last hour had come, and there was no help for him in this world; and the spray shaken from the billows blinded him, the great walls of water crumbled over him; strength failed him, and his memory ceased to picture images of the old time﻿—his heart to beat with ambition; and to keep the weight of his head above the surface was becoming a thing worth the ransom of kings. As he was sinking and turning his eyes upward, he heard a flutter as of fledgling’s wings, and the two red ruby eyes of the hawk were visible above him, like steady fires in the gloom. And the hawk perched on him, and buried itself among the wet hairs of his head, and presently taking the Identical in its beak, the hawk lifted him half out of water, and bore him a distance, and dropped him. This the hawk did many times, and at the last, Shibli Bagarag felt land beneath him, and could wade through the surges to the shore. He gave thanks to the Supreme Disposer, kneeling prostrate on the shore, and fell into a sleep deep in peacefulness as a fathomless well, unruffled by a breath.
Now, when it was dawn Shibli Bagarag awoke and looked inland, and saw plainly the minarets of a city shining in the first beams, and the front of yellow mountains, and people moving about the walls and on the towers and among the pastures round the city; so he made toward them, and inquired of them the name of their city. And they stared at him, crying, “What! know’st thou not the City of Oolb? the hawk on thy shoulder could tell thee that much.” He looked and saw that the hawk was on his shoulder; and its left wing was scorched, the plumage blackened. So he said to the hawk, “Is it profitable, O preserving bird, to ask of thee questions?”
The hawk shook its wings and closed an eye.
So he said, “Do I well in entering this city?”
The hawk shook its wings again and closed an eye.
So he said, “To what house shall I direct my steps in this strange city for the attainment of the purpose I have?”
The hawk flew, and soared, and alighted on the topmost of the towers of Oolb. So when it returned he said, “O bird! rare bird! my counsellor! it is an indication, this alighting on the highest tower, that thou advisest me to go straight to the palace of the King?”

===Chinese===

当他浮出水面时，那艘巨船已荡然无存，原处唯余一片漆黑；他拼命与巨浪搏击，心想大限已至，尘世间再无任何力量能拯救于他；波涛溅起的水雾迷盲了他的双眼，高耸的水墙崩塌压顶；他气力耗尽，脑海中不再浮现往昔的记忆，胸中不再跳动着建功立业的雄心；连将沉重的头颅勉强托出水面，都成了纵有万乘之君的赎金亦难以企及的奢望。正当他渐渐下沉、绝望仰望苍穹之际，他听见了一阵雏鸟振翅般的扑棱声，两颗如红宝石般的血红鹰眼在昏暗中浮现，宛若永不熄灭的幽火。苍鹰落在他身上，钻入他头顶湿漉漉的发丝之中，随后用利喙衔住独一神发（the Identical），将他半个身躯生生拽出水面，驮负着他飞越一段距离，再将他放下。苍鹰如此反复多次，终于，希布利·巴加拉格感到脚下踩到了坚实的陆地，能够涉水穿过汹涌的浪花登上海岸。他虔诚跪倒在沙滩上，向万物的主宰俯首叩拜感恩，随后沉沉睡去，宁谧深沉宛若一口无底深井，不起一丝波澜。
到了破晓时分，希布利·巴加拉格醒转过来，举目向内陆望去，只见在初升朝霞中耸立着一座城池的宣礼塔，泛着金黄的崇山峻岭横亘眼前，城墙上、塔楼间以及城池周遭的草场上皆有人影走动；于是他朝众人走去，向他们打听这座城池的名号。人们瞪大双眼望着他，高呼道：“怎么！你难道连乌尔布城（City of Oolb）都不知道吗？单凭你肩头落着的那只苍鹰便能告诉你这一节。”他扭头一看，只见苍鹰正立在自己的肩头；它的左翼已被烈火灼伤，羽毛焦黑。于是他向苍鹰问道：“救命的神鸟啊，向你请教可会有所裨益？”
苍鹰抖动了一下双翼，眨了眨一只眼睛。
他又问：“我进入此城可是明智之举？”
苍鹰再次抖了抖双翼，眨了一只眼睛。
他又问：“在这座陌生的城池中，我当迈步前往何门何宅，方能成就我心中的宏愿？”
苍鹰振翅腾空高飞，最终轻盈栖息在乌尔布最高的塔楼之巅。待它飞回肩头，他问道：“奇鸟啊！世间罕匹的神鸟、我的谋士！栖息在最高塔楼之上，莫非是在指引我径直前往国王的王宫？”

===Original===

The hawk flapped its wings and winked both eyes; so Shibli Bagarag took forth the phial from his breast, remembering the virtues of the waters of the Well of Paravid, and touched his lips with them, that he might be endowed with flowing speech before the King of Oolb. As he did this the phial was open, and the hawk leaned to it and dipped its beak into the water; and he entered the city and passed through the long streets towards the palace of the King, and craved audience of him as one that had a thing marvellous to tell. So the King commanded that Shibli Bagarag should be brought before him, for he was a lover of marvels. As he went into the presence of the King, Shibli Bagarag listened to the hawk, for the hawk spake his language, and it said, “Proclaim to the King a new wonder﻿—‘the talking hawk.’ ”
So when he had bent his body to the King, he proclaimed the new wonder; and the King seemed not to observe the hawk, and said, “From what city art thou?”
He answered, “Native, O King, to Shiraz; newly from the City of Shagpat.”
And the King asked, “How is it with that hairy wonder?”
He answered, “The dark forest flourisheth about him.”
And the King said, “That is well! We of the City of Oolb take our fashions from them of the City of Shagpat, and it is but yesterday that I bastinadoed a barber that strayed among us.”
Shibli Bagarag sighed when he heard the King, and thought to himself, “How unfortunate is the race of barbers, once honourable and in esteem! Surely it will not be otherwise till Shagpat is shaved!” And the King called out to him for the cause of his sighing; so he said, “I sigh, O King of the age, considering how like may be the case of the barber bastinadoed but yesterday, in his worth and value, to that of Roomdroom, the reader of planets, that was a barber.”
And he related the story of Roomdroom for the edification of the King and the exaltation of barbercraft, delivering himself neatly and winningly and pointedly, so that the story should apply, which was its merit and its origin.

===Chinese===

苍鹰扑动双翼，同时眨了眨双眼；希布利·巴加拉格想起帕拉维德古井泉水（the waters of the Well of Paravid）的奇效，从怀中掏出小药瓶，以泉水轻触嘴唇，好教自己在乌尔布国王面前拥有口若悬河的善辩口才。就在他开启药瓶之际，苍鹰探过头来，将利喙伸入瓶中饮了一滴泉水；随后他迈步进城，穿过长长的街巷走向王宫，以身怀绝妙奇闻之人的身份请求觐见。国王平生最喜奇观异闻，当即降旨宣召希布利·巴加拉格觐见。在步入御前之际，希布利·巴加拉格侧耳倾听苍鹰的低语，皆因苍鹰此刻已能口吐人言，只听它说道：“向国王启奏一桩旷世奇闻——‘会说话的苍鹰’。”
待他向国王躬身行礼之后，便奏禀了这一奇观；国王似乎并未留心肩头的苍鹰，问道：“你来自哪座城池？”
他答道：“回禀国王，我原籍设拉子（Shiraz）；刚自沙格帕特城（City of Shagpat）而来。”
国王问道：“那位多毛的奇人近况如何？”
他答道：“他周身的幽暗须发密林生长得繁茂昌盛。”
国王道：“善哉！我们乌尔布城向来以沙格帕特城为潮流风尚，就在昨日，我还亲手降旨用藤杖狠狠鞭笞了一个误入此地的流浪理发师。”
希布利·巴加拉格闻听此言不由长叹一声，暗自思忖：“曾几何时受人尊崇礼敬的理发师一脉，如今何其命途多舛！在沙格帕特被彻底剃光之前，世道怕是绝难扭转了！”国王大声喝问他为何叹息；于是他奏道：“当世之君啊，我之所以叹息，乃是想到昨日遭乱杖鞭笞的那位理发师，其品格与价值或许正如曾为理发师的观星术士鲁姆德鲁姆（Roomdroom）那般卓绝。”
随后，为了启迪国王并重振理发行当的无上荣光，他娓娓道来鲁姆德鲁姆的传奇故事，谈吐优雅流利、风趣巧妙且切中要害，使得故事紧扣时宜，这正是此篇故事的精妙与源起。

## The Thwackings / 乱杖痛打
===Original===

It was ordained that Shibli Bagarag, nephew to the renowned Baba Mustapha, chief barber to the Court of Persia, should shave Shagpat, the son of Shimpoor, the son of Shoolpi, the son of Shullum; and they had been clothiers for generations, even to the time of Shagpat, the illustrious.
Now, the story of Shibli Bagarag, and of the ball he followed, and of the subterranean kingdom he came to, and of the enchanted palace he entered, and of the sleeping king he shaved, and of the two princesses he released, and of the afreet held in subjection by the arts of one and bottled by her, is it not known as ’twere written on the fingernails of men and traced in their corner-robes? As the poet says:

> “Ripe with oft telling and old is the tale,

But ’tis of the sort that can never grow stale.”

Now, things were in that condition with Shibli Bagarag, that on a certain day he was hungry and abject, and the city of Shagpat the clothier was before him; so he made toward it, deliberating as to how he should procure a meal, for he had not a dirham in his girdle, and the remembrance of great dishes and savoury ingredients were to him as the illusion of rivers sheening on the sands to travellers gasping with thirst.
And he considered his case, crying, “Surely this comes of wandering, and ’tis the curse of the inquiring spirit! for in Shiraz, where my craft is in favour, I should be sitting now with my uncle, Baba Mustapha, the loquacious one, cross-legged, partaking of seasoned sweet dishes, dipping my fingers in them, rejoicing my soul with scandal of the Court!”
Now, he came to a knoll of sand under a palm, from which the yellow domes and mosques of the city of Shagpat, and its black cypresses, and marble palace fronts, and shining pillars, and lofty carven arches that spanned half-circles of the hot grey sky, were plainly visible. Then gazed he awhile despondingly on the city of Shagpat, and groaned in contemplation of his evil plight, as is said by the poet:

> “The curse of sorrow is comparison!

As the sun casteth shade, night showeth star,

We, measuring what we were by what we are,

Behold the depth to which we are undone.”

Wherefore he counselleth:

> “Look neither too much up, nor down at all,

But, forward stepping, strive no more to fall.”

And the advice is excellent; but, as is again said:

> “The preacher preacheth, and the hearer heareth,

===Chinese===

宿命注定，波斯宫廷首席理发师、大名鼎鼎的巴巴·穆斯塔法（Baba Mustapha）的侄儿希布利·巴加拉格（Shibli Bagarag），终将剃光舒伦之子舒尔皮之子欣普尔之子沙格帕特（Shagpat）；他们世世代代皆为布商，直至闻名遐迩的沙格帕特这一代。
至于希布利·巴加拉格追随神奇弹丸的传奇、他所抵达的地下王国、步入的迷境王宫、剃光须发的沉睡君王、解救的两位公主，以及被其中一位公主施展巫术降伏并装入魔瓶的恶灵（afreet），世人岂非耳熟能详，犹如铭刻在指甲之上、绣在衣袂之角一般尽人皆知？正如诗人所云：

> “故事常道虽古老，
> 篇章百诵永不朽。”

然而当时希布利·巴加拉格的处境却极其狼狈，某日他腹中空空、落魄潦倒，布商沙格帕特的城池已然近在眼前；他举步朝城池走去，心中不住盘算着如何谋求一顿饭食，皆因他腰带里连一枚迪拉姆银币（dirham）都掏不出来，对佳肴美馔与浓郁滋味的回忆，在他眼中宛如干渴得喘不过气来的旅人所见的茫茫沙海幻影清流。
他思量着自身的境遇，失声叹道：“这全怪我四处流浪，实乃探幽求奇天性招致的诅咒！若在设拉子（Shiraz）这般尊崇我手艺的故土，我此刻本该与我那位饶舌的叔父巴巴·穆斯塔法盘腿对坐，细品香甜诱人的各色甜点，伸手蘸食，畅谈宫廷秘闻以悦心神！”
此时，他来到一株棕榈树下的一处沙丘，从此处极目远眺，沙格帕特城那金黄的穹顶、清真寺、黝黑的柏树、大理石宫殿门楼、晶莹的石柱，以及横跨在焦灼灰蒙天空半空的巍峨雕花拱门，皆清晰可见。他沮丧地凝望着沙格帕特城，抚今追昔，为自己的凄惨境地哀叹连连，正如诗人所云：

> “悲伤之诅咒，莫过于攀比！
> 烈日投浓荫，暗夜显明星；
> 忆往昔峥嵘，比今日潦倒，
> 始知坠深渊，万劫难复兴。”

因此诗人劝诫道：

> “莫过仰首望，亦莫俯首叹；
> 稳健阔步前，谨防再跌翻。”

这番劝诫固然精妙绝伦；但正如古人又云：

> “说教者空谈，倾听者旁闻；

===Original===

But comfort first each function requireth.”

And “wisdom to a hungry stomach is thin pottage,” saith the shrewd reader of men. Little comfort was there with Shibli Bagarag, as he looked on the city of Shagpat the clothier! He cried aloud that his evil chance had got the better of him, and rolled his body in the sand, beating his breast, and conjuring up images of the profusion of dainties and the abundance of provision in Shiraz, exclaiming, “Well-a-way and woe’s me! this it is to be selected for the diversion of him that plotteth against man.” Truly is it written:

> “On different heads misfortunes come:

One bears them firm, another faints,

While this one hangs them like a drum

Whereon to batter loud complaints.”

And of the three kinds, they who bang the drum outnumber the silent ones as do the billows of the sea the ships that swim, or the grains of sand the trees that grow; a noisy multitude.
Now, he was in the pits of despondency, even as one that yieldeth without further struggle to the waves of tempest at midnight, when he was ware of one standing over him﻿—a woman, old, wrinkled, a very crone, with but room for the drawing of a thread between her nose and her chin; she was, as is cited of them who betray the doings of Time,

> “Wrinkled at the rind, and overripe at the core,”

===Chinese===

肚肠若不暖，万事皆难行。”

洞悉人性的智者曾言：“对于饥肠辘辘的空腹而言，大智慧不过是一碗稀薄的烂粥。”凝望着布商沙格帕特的那座城池，希布利·巴加拉格心中寻不到半点慰藉！他哀嚎厄运彻底压垮了自己，在黄沙中翻滚身躯，捶胸顿足，脑海中不住浮现出设拉子琳琅满目的佳肴与丰盛的供给，哭喊道：“呜呼哀哉，痛煞我也！这便是沦为捉弄凡人之黑手取乐消遣的下场。”诚如古训所云：

> “厄运临头各不同：
> 有人咬牙硬支撑，
> 有人瘫软倒在地，
> 亦有人将其作皮鼓，
> 擂起震天怨怼声。”

在世间这三类人中，那擂鼓抱怨之辈的数目，远胜于默默忍受之人，正如怒海波涛远多于漂游的航船、沙海尘埃远多于破土的树木；实乃嘈杂喧嚣的芸芸众生。
正当他深陷绝望的深渊、宛若子夜暴风雨中彻底放弃挣扎任由惊涛吞没的罹难之人时，他忽然察觉到有人正伫立在他上方——那是一个身形干瘪、满脸褶皱的老妪，鼻尖与下巴之间紧凑得几乎容不下一根细线穿过；正如形容岁月残酷侵蚀的诗句所云：

> “皮壳起千褶，果心已烂熟，”

===Original===

and every part of her nodded and shook like a tree sapped by the waters, and her joints were sharp as the hind-legs of a grasshopper; she was indeed one close-wrecked upon the rocks of Time.
Now, when the old woman had scanned Shibli Bagarag, she called to him, “O thou! what is it with thee, that thou rollest as one reft of his wits?”
He answered her, “I bewail my condition, which is beggary, and the lack of that which filleth with pleasantness.”
So the old woman said, “Tell me thy case.”
He answered her, “O old woman, surely it was written at my birth that I should take ruin from the readers of planets. Now, they proclaimed that I was one day destined for great things, if I stood by my tackle, I, a barber. Know then, that I have had many offers and bribes, seductive ones, from the rich and the exalted in rank; and I heeded them not, mindful of what was foretold of me. I stood by my tackle as a warrior standeth by his arms, flourishing them. Now, when I found great things came not to me, and ’twas the continuance of sameness and satiety with Baba Mustapha, my uncle, in Shiraz﻿—the tongue-wagger, the endless tattler﻿—surely I was advised by the words of the poet to go forth in search of what was wanting, and he says:

> “ ‘Thou that dreamest an Event,

While Circumstance is but a waste of sand,

Arise, take up thy fortunes in thy hand,

And daily forward pitch thy tent.’

===Chinese===

她浑身上下不住颤抖摇晃，宛如被水流掏空根基的朽树，关节干瘪突兀得好似蚱蜢的后足；委实是在岁月的礁石上被撞得粉身碎骨的残躯。
老妪仔细打量了希布利·巴加拉格片刻，向他喊道：“喂，你！你究竟遭遇了何事，竟像个失心疯一般在沙地里打滚？”
他答道：“我自哀自怜身陷行乞之境，腹中缺乏充饥果腹之物。”
老妪道：“同我说说你的遭际。”
他答道：“老婆子，想来在我出世之际，宿命便注定我要被那些观星术士害得倾家荡产。当年他们预言，只要我坚守自己的行当器具，身为理发师的我终有一日能成就旷世伟业。须知，曾有许多达官贵人向我许以重金厚禄、极尽诱惑之能事；可我心系当年的预言，皆未予理睬。我坚守着自己的理发器具，宛若勇士紧握兵刃、挥舞耀武。然而，眼见伟业迟迟未曾降临，在设拉子同我那饶舌多嘴、废话连篇的叔父巴巴·穆斯塔法日复一日单调消磨，我便听从了诗人的劝诫，离乡远行去求索缺失之物，正如诗人所言：

> ‘怀揣伟业之宏愿，
> 处境纵然是沙洲，
> 亦当奋起握机运，
> 日日扎营向远猷。’

===Original===

“Now, I passed from city to city, proclaiming my science, holding aloft my tackle. *Wullahy!* many adventures were mine, and if there’s some day propitiousness in fortune, O old woman, I’ll tell thee of what befell me in the kingdom of Shah Shamshureen: ’tis wondrous, a matter to draw down the lower jaw with amazement! Now, so it was, that in the eyes of one city I was honoured and in request, by reason of my calling, and I fared sumptuously, even as a great officer of state surrounded by slaves, lounging upon clouds of silk stuffs, circled by attentive ears: in another city there was no beast so base as I. Wah! I was one hunted of men and an abomination; no housing for me, nought to operate upon. I was the lean dog that lieth in wait for offal. It seemeth certain, O old woman, that a curse hath fallen on barbercraft in these days, because of the Identical, whose might I know not. Everywhere it is growing in disrepute; ’tis languishing! Nevertheless till now I have preserved my tackle, and I would descend on yonder city to exercise it, even for a livelihood, forgetting awhile great things, but that I dread men may have changed there also﻿—and there’s no stability in them, I call Allah (whose name be praised!) to witness; so should I be a thing unsightly, subject to hateful castigation; wherefore is it that I am in that state described by the poet, when,

> “ ‘Dreading retreat, dreading advance to make,

Round we revolve, like to the wounded snake.’

===Chinese===

“于是我辗转于一座座城池之间，宣扬我的手艺，高擎我的行当器具。凭安拉起誓（Wullahy）！我经历了诸般奇遇，老婆子，若有朝一日时来运转，我定要将我在沙阿·沙姆舒林（Shah Shamshureen）王国中的奇遇讲给你听：委实惊心动魄，足以教人惊得合不拢嘴！然而世事难料，在某座城池之中，我因手艺备受礼遇追捧，享用着珍馐美馔，宛若被奴仆环侍的达官显贵，斜倚在锦缎软榻之上，四下皆是屏息恭听的耳朵；可在另一座城池中，世间走兽亦未有如我这般卑贱者。哇！我成了人人喊打的过街老鼠、污秽之物；既无立足之所，亦无人让我动刀理发。我沦落成了伺机抢食残羹剩饭的瘦狗。老婆子，如今看来，皆因独一神发（the Identical）那不知名的魔力作祟，理发行当近年来定是遭受了天谴。这门手艺在各地声名狼藉，日渐衰颓！即便如此，至今我仍完好保全着理发器械，我本想进城操持旧业，哪怕只为糊口度日，暂且忘却旷世伟业，可我又唯恐那座城里的人亦已变了风气——世人善变难测，我指着安拉（赞美祂的圣名！）起誓；真怕自己又沦为遭人嫌恶的异类，饱受皮肉刑戮之苦；正因如此，我才陷入了诗人笔下的窘境：

> ‘退缩心惶惶，前行意怯怯，
> 盘桓难自主，恰似负伤蛇。’

===Original===

“Is not my case now a piteous one, one that toucheth the tender corner in man and woman?”
When she that listened had heard him to an end, she shook her garments, crying, “O youth, son of my uncle, be comforted! for, if it is as I think, the readers of planets were right, and thou art thus early within reach of great things﻿—nigh grasping them.”
Then she fell to mumbling and reciting jigs of verse, quaint measures; and she pored along the sand to where a line had been drawn, and saw that the footprints of the youth were traced along it. Lo, at that sight she clapped her hands joyfully, and ran up to the youth, and peered in his face, exclaiming, “Great things indeed! and praise thou the readers of planets, O nephew of the barber, they that sent thee searching the Event thou art to master. *Wullahy!* have I not half a mind to call thee already Master of the Event?”
Then she abated somewhat in her liveliness, and said to him, “Know that the city thou seest is the city of Shagpat, the clothier, and there’s no one living on the face of Earth, nor a soul that requireth thy craft more than he. Go therefore thou, bold of heart, brisk, full of the sprightliness of the barber, and enter to him. Lo, thou’lt see him lolling in his shopfront to be admired of this people﻿—marvelled at. Oh! no mistaking of Shagpat, and the mole might discern Shagpat among myriads of our kind; and enter thou to him gaily, as to perform a friendly office, one meriting thanks and gratulations, saying, ‘I will preserve thee the Identical!’ Now he’ll at first feign not to understand thee, dense of wit that he is! but mince not matters with him, perform well thy operation, and thou wilt come to great things. What say I? ’tis certain that when thou hast shaved Shagpat thou wilt have achieved the greatest of things, and be most noteworthy of thy race, thou, Shibli Bagarag, even thou! and thou wilt be Master of the Event, so named in anecdotes and histories and records, to all succeeding generations.”
At her words the breast of Shibli Bagarag took in a great wind, and he hung his head a moment to ponder them; and he thought, “There’s provokingness in the speech of this old woman, and she’s one that instigateth keenly. She called me by my name! Heard I that? ’Tis a mystery!” And he thought, “Peradventure she is a genie, one of an ill tribe, and she’s luring me to my perdition in this city!

===Chinese===

“我如今的遭遇难道不够凄惨、不足以触动天下男女的恻隐柔肠吗？”
静听他诉说完这番话的老妪抖了抖身上的衣衫，高声喊道：“少年啊，我的堂弟，放宽心吧！依我看，那些观星术士说得一点没错，你如今年纪轻轻，便已触碰到了旷世伟业的门槛——甚至唾手可得。”
说罢，她开始念念有词，哼唱起古怪成趣的短歌小曲；她顺着沙地凝视着划出的一道线条，发现青年的脚印正沿着那道痕迹延伸。见此情景，她欣喜若狂地拍起双手，奔到青年跟前，死死打量着他的脸庞，高呼道：“委实是旷世伟业！你当盛赞那些观星术士，理发师的侄儿啊，正是他们指引你去求索注定由你降伏的命定大事（the Event）。凭安拉起誓（Wullahy）！我都忍不住想现在就称呼你为命定大事的主宰（Master of the Event）了！”
随后她收敛了几分活泼，正色对他道：“可知眼前这座城池，乃是布商沙格帕特的城池，世间绝无任何凡人比他更需要你的手艺了。因此你当鼓足勇气，脚下生风，带着理发师特有的敏捷与利落去见他。瞧，你定会见他仰卧在自家的店铺门前，受城中万民的钦羡与惊叹。噢！沙格帕特绝不会认错，纵使鼹鼠也能从千万人中一眼辨认出沙格帕特；你且欢欢喜喜迎上前去，权当替他尽一份值得感激与称颂的邻里友谊，对他说：‘我愿为你保全独一神发（the Identical）！’起初他定会装傻充愣，皆因他生性顽钝！但你切莫同他兜圈子，好生施展你的剃头绝技，你便能成就通天大业。我还需多言吗？毫无疑问，一旦你将沙格帕特剃个精光，你便成就了世间最伟大的伟业，成为族人中最为显赫的人物，你，希布利·巴加拉格，正是你！你将成为名垂青史、万代传诵的命定大事的主宰。”
听闻此言，希布利·巴加拉格胸中涌起一股浩然豪气，他低头沉思片刻，暗想：“这老婆子的话语中带着一股撩拨人心的力量，煽动人心极具能耐。她竟然能叫出我的名讳！我方才听错了吗？当真离奇！”他又想：“莫非她是一尊出身邪祟部落的神怪，故意诱我进城自取灭亡不成！

===Original===

How if that be so?” And again he thought, “It cannot be! She’s probably the genie that presided over my birth, and promised me dower of great things through the mouths of the readers of planets.”
Now, when Shibli Bagarag had so deliberated, he lifted his sight, and lo, the old woman was no longer before him! He stared, and rubbed his eyes, but she was clean gone. Then ran he to the knolls and eminences that were scattered about, to command a view, but she was nowhere visible. So he thought, “ ’Twas a dream!” and he was composing himself to despair upon the scant herbage of one of those knolls, when as he chanced to gaze down the city below, he saw there a commotion and a crowd of people flocking one way; he thought, “ ’Twas surely no dream? come not genii, and go they not, in the fashion of that old woman? I’ll even descend on yonder city, and try my tackle on Shagpat, inquiring for him, and if he is there, I shall know I have had to do with a potent spirit. Allah protect me!”
So, having shut together the clasps of resolve, he arose and made for the gates of the city, and entered it by the principal entrance. It was a fair city, the fairest and chief of that country; prosperous, powerful; a mart for numerous commodities, handicrafts, wares; round it a wild country and a waste of sand, ruled by the lion in his wrath, and in it the tiger, the camelopard, the antelope, and other animals. Hither, in caravans, came the people of Oolb and the people of Damascus, and the people of Vatz, and they of Baghdad, and the Ringheez, great traders, and others, trading; and there was constant flow of intercourse between them and the city of Shagpat. Now as Shibli Bagarag paced up one of the streets of the city, he beheld a multitude in procession following one that was crowned after the manner of kings, with a glittering crown, clad in the yellow girdled robes, and he sporting a fine profusion of hair, unequalled by all around him, save by one that was a little behind, shadowed by his presence. So Shibli Bagarag thought, “Is one of this twain Shagpat? for never till now have I seen such rare growths, and ’twere indeed a bliss to slip the blade between them and those masses of darkness that hang from them.”

===Chinese===

倘若真是如此，又该当如何？”转念他又想：“绝无可能！她想必是当年执掌我降生的守护神怪，曾借由观星术士之口许诺赐予我无上大业。”
希布利·巴加拉格如此权衡一番之后，抬头一瞧，看哪，老妪已不在眼前！他瞪大双眼揉了揉眼睛，可她早已踪迹全无。他急忙奔上散布在周遭的高坡丘陵四下眺望，却见四下空无一人。他不由心想：“方才不过是一场梦罢了！”他正欲躺倒在一处沙丘稀疏的草丛中听任绝望摆布，不经意间低头俯瞰下方的城池，只见城中人群骚动，成群结队的百姓正朝着同一个方向涌去；他暗忖：“那定然不是梦境！神怪来去无踪，岂非正是那老婆子的模样？我且下山进城，寻访沙格帕特，用我的器具在他头上试上一试，若当真有此人，我便知晓自己遇上了神通广大的神明。愿安拉庇佑我！”
于是他下定决心，翻身站起奔向城门，由正门大步迈入城内。那是一座繁华绝丽的城池，堪称该国首屈一指的首善之区；富庶昌盛，强悍威武；乃是汇聚四方百工百货、各色货物的贸易重镇；城池周遭乃是广袤蛮荒的瀚海沙原，由暴怒的雄狮统御，其间出没着猛虎、长颈鹿、羚羊与各色猛兽。来自乌尔布（Oolb）、大马士革（Damascus）、瓦茨（Vatz）、巴格达（Baghdad）以及赫赫有名的大商人林吉兹人（Ringheez）等各路商旅，结成驼队来此通商贸易；商贾往来络绎不绝，与沙格帕特城互通有无。正当希布利·巴加拉格漫步在城中一条大街上时，只见浩浩荡荡的人群正簇拥着一位仿若帝王般头戴灿烂王冠、身披黄袍玉带之人巡游，那人炫耀着一头浓密无双的繁茂须发，在场众人无一人堪与之相比，唯有稍落后其身后、被其威势笼罩的一人或可匹敌。希布利·巴加拉格心想：“这二人中莫非有一人便是沙格帕特？皆因我生平从未见过这般惊人的发量，若能以利刃插入其间、将那垂挂的一团漆黑彻底削去，委实是人生至乐。”

===Original===

Then he stepped before the King, and made himself prominent in his path, humbling himself; and it was as he anticipated, the King prevented his removal by the slaves that would have dragged him away, and desired a hearing as to his business, and what brought him to the city, a stranger.
Thereupon Shibli Bagarag prostrated himself and cried, “O great King, Sovereign of the Time! surely I am one to be looked on with the eye of grace; and I am nephew to Baba Mustapha, renowned in Shiraz, a barber;﻿—I a barber, and it is my prayer, O King of the Age, that thou take me under thy protection and the shield of thy fair will, while I perform good work in this city by operating on the unshorn.”
When he had spoken, the King made a point of his eyebrows, and exclaimed, “Shiraz? So they hold out against Shagpat yet, aha? Shiraz! that nest of them! that reptile’s nest!” Then he turned to his Vizier beside him, and said, “What shall be done with this fellow?”
So the Vizier replied, “ ’Twere well, O King, he be summoned to a sense of the loathsomeness of his craft by the agency of fifty stripes.”
The King said, “ ’Tis commanded!”
Then he passed forward in his majesty, and Shibli Bagarag was ware of the power of five slaves upon him, and he was hurried at a quick pace through the streets and before the eyes of the people, even to the common receptacle of felons, and there received from each slave severally ten thwacks with a thong: ’tis certain that at every thwack the thong took an airing before it descended upon him. Then loosed they him, to wander whither he listed; and disgust was strong in him by reason of the disgrace and the severity of the administration of the blows. He strayed along the streets in wretchedness, and hunger increased on him, assailing him first as a wolf in his vitals, then as it had been a chasm yawning betwixt his trunk and his lower members. And he thought, “I have been long in chase of great things, and the hope of attaining them is great; yet, *wullahy!* would I barter all for one refreshing meal, and the sense of fullness. ’Tis so, and sad is it!” And he was mindful of the poet’s words﻿—

> “Who seeks the shadow to the substance sinneth,

And daily craving what is not, he thinneth:

His lean ambition how shall he attain?

For with this constant foolishness he doeth,

He, waxing liker to what he pursueth,

===Chinese===

于是他跨步拦在国王仪仗之前，卑躬屈膝地挡在御道正中；果然正如他所料，国王喝止了欲将他拖走的奴仆，命人询问这异乡客的来意与底细。
希布利·巴加拉格当即匍匐叩拜，高呼道：“伟大的君王、当世的至尊啊！我实乃堪受天恩垂青之人；我是设拉子闻名遐迩的理发师巴巴·穆斯塔法的侄儿；我自己亦是一名理发师，当世之君啊，我祈求蒙受陛下的庇佑与隆恩，准许我在本城施展绝技，为未经剃度之人削发修面。”
听罢此言，国王倒竖双眉，厉声喝道：“设拉子？那帮厮鸟至今还在对抗沙格帕特，啊哈？设拉子！那帮爬虫的贼窝！那帮鼠辈的巢穴！”随后他转向身旁的宰相（Vizier），问道：“当如何处置这厮？”
宰相答道：“启奏陛下，理当赏他五十记皮鞭（fifty stripes），好教他痛改前非，认定理发手艺何等肮脏下贱。”
国王旨意一下：“准奏！”
言罢，国王端着尊严仪仗昂首前行，希布利·巴加拉格立时领教了五名奴仆（five slaves）的蛮力，被他们在众目睽睽之下飞速拖过街道，径直押往关押罪犯的刑场，在那里由每名奴仆分别用皮鞭狠狠抽打了他十记（ten thwacks with a thong）：千真万确，每抽一鞭之前，那皮鞭皆在半空中呼啸挥舞一圈方才狠狠砸落在他背上。打完之后，他们将他随手释放，任其四处游荡；奇耻大辱与剧烈的皮肉之痛令他心中充满了无尽的厌恶。他失魂落魄地游荡在街巷之间，腹中的饥渴越发猛烈，起初如饿狼般啃噬着他的五脏六腑，随后仿佛在躯干与下肢之间裂开了一道万丈深渊。他暗忖：“我苦苦追寻旷世伟业多时，成就伟业的希冀固然浩大；然而凭安拉起誓（wullahy）！此刻我宁愿拿一切去换一顿饱饭与果腹的充实。委实如此，何其凄凉！”他不由忆起了诗人的训诫：

> “弃本逐影实大过，
> 妄求虚妄日消磨；
> 骨瘦如柴慕虚荣，
> 如此痴顽复何获？
> 渐似所逐幻影形，

===Original===

Himself becometh what he chased in vain!”

And again:

> “Of honour half my fellows boast﻿—

A thing that scorns and kills us:

Methinks that honours us the most

Which nourishes and fills us.”

So he thought he would of a surety fling far away his tackle, discard barbercraft, and be as other men, a mortal, forgotten with his generation. And he cried aloud, “O thou old woman! thou deceiver! what halt thou obtained for me by thy deceits? and why put I faith in thee to the purchase of a thwacking? Woe’s me! I would thou hadst been but a dream, thou crone! thou guileful parcel of belabouring bones!”
Now, while he lounged and strolled, and was abusing the old woman, he looked before him, and lo, one lolling in his shopfront, and people standing outside the shop, marking him with admiration and reverence, and pointing him out to each other with approving gestures. He who lolled there was indeed a miracle of hairiness, black with hair as he had been muzzled with it, and his head as it were a berry in a bush by reason of it. Then thought Shibli Bagarag, “ ’Tis Shagpat! If the mole could swear to him, surely can I.” So he regarded the clothier, and there was naught seen on Earth like the gravity of Shagpat as he lolled before those people, that failed not to assemble in groups and gaze at him. He was as a sleepy lion cased in his mane; as an owl drowsy in the daylight. Now would he close an eye, or move two fingers, but of other motion made he none, yet the people gazed at him with eagerness. Shibli Bagarag was astonished at them, thinking, “Hair! hair! There is might in hair; but there is greater might in the barber! Nevertheless here the barber is scorned, the grower of crops held in amazing reverence.” Then thought he, “ ’Tis truly wondrous the crop he groweth; not even King Shamshureen, after a thousand years, sported such mighty profusion! Him I sheared: it was a high task!﻿—why not this Shagpat?”
Now, long gazing on Shagpat awoke in Shibli Bagarag fierce desire to shear him, and it was scarce in his power to restrain himself from flying at the clothier, he saying, “What obstacle now? what protecteth him? Nay, why not trust to the old woman? Said she not I should first essay on Shagpat? and ’twas my folly in appealing to the King that brought on me that thwacking. ’Tis well! I’ll trust to her words. *Wullahy!* will it not lead me to great things?”

===Chinese===

终成镜花水月客！”

诗人又云：

> “世人夸耀虚名节，
> 辱人杀人皆由此；
> 我道至尊之荣耀，
> 莫若暖衣饱肚皮。”

于是他暗下决心，定要将理发器具狠狠抛弃，彻底告别理发行当，像寻常凡人一样碌碌一生，随同辈之人一同归于尘土。他失声痛骂道：“你这该死的老婆子！女骗子！你的谎言究竟替我换来了什么？我何苦听信你的鬼话平白挨了一顿乱杖痛打？痛煞我也！我真巴不得你只是一场幻梦，你这老妖婆！你这具招来乱棍笞打的狡诈枯骨！”
正当他游荡漫步、不住痛骂老妪之际，抬头向前一望，看哪！一人正惬意仰卧在自家的店铺门前，门外围拢着大批百姓，满怀崇敬与钦羡地端详着他，指指点点交口称赞。那仰卧之人真乃多毛的旷世奇迹，满面长满浓密的黑毛，宛如戴着一副厚重的毛口套，整个头颅因毛发过于繁密，活像荆棘灌木丛中包裹的一颗小小浆果。希布利·巴加拉格暗想：“这定是沙格帕特（Shagpat）！若鼹鼠都能认出他，我自然更能一眼看穿。”他仔细打量着布商，只见沙格帕特在聚拢围观的人群面前仰卧的神态，其沉稳庄重世间绝无仅有。他恰似一头深陷浓密鬃毛中昏昏欲睡的雄狮；又似白昼里打盹的猫头鹰。他时而合上一只眼，时而动弹两根手指，除此以外别无任何动静，可围观的百姓却看得津津有味、如痴如醉。希布利·巴加拉格见状大为诧异，心想：“毛发！毛发！毛发固然有威能；但理发师的本领远在毛发之上！可在此地理发师遭人唾弃，蓄发之徒反倒受人顶礼膜拜。”随后他又想：“他蓄起的毛发委实惊人；纵使沙姆舒林国王（King Shamshureen）历经千年（a thousand years），也未曾炫耀过这等惊人的茂盛须发！我尚且剃光了那位国王：那可是通天大业！——为何剃不得眼前的沙格帕特？”
久久凝视着沙格帕特，希布利·巴加拉格胸中重新燃起了要将他剃光的狂热欲望，他几乎难以克制扑向布商的冲动，自语道：“如今还有何阻碍？有何神力护佑着他？何不信信那老婆子的话？她此前不是明言让我先对沙格帕特下手吗？全怪我犯蠢去向国王请愿，才招致了那顿毒打。甚好！我便信她这一回。凭安拉起誓（Wullahy）！这难道不会引领我成就旷世大业吗？”

===Original===

So it was, that as he thought this he continued to keep eye on Shagpat, and the hunger that was in him passed, and became a ravenous vulture that flew from him and singled forth Shagpat as prey; and there was no help for it but in he must go and state his case to Shagpat, and essay shearing him.
Now, when he was in the presence, he exclaimed, “Peace, O vendor of apparel, unto thee and unto thine!”
Shagpat answered, “That with thee!”
Said Shibli Bagarag, “I have heard of thee, O thou wonder! *Wullahy!* I am here to render homage to that I behold.”
Shagpat answered, “ ’Tis well!”
Then said Shibli Bagarag, “Praise my discretion! I have even this day entered the city, and it is to thee I offer the first shave, O tangle of glory!”
At these words Shagpat darkened, saying gruffly, “Thy jest is offensive, and it is unseasonable for staleness and lack of holiness.”
But Shibli Bagarag cried, “No jest, O purveyor to the outward of us! but a very excellent earnest.”
Thereat the face of Shagpat was as an exceeding red berry in a bush, and he said angrily, “Have done! no more of it! or haply my spleen will be awakened, and that of them who see with more eyes than two.”
Nevertheless Shibli Bagarag urged him, and he winked, and gesticulated, and pointed to his head, crying, “Fall not, O man of the nicety of measure, into the trap of error; for ’tis I that am a barber, and a rarity in this city, even Shibli Bagarag of Shiraz! Know me nephew of the renowned Baba Mustapha, chief barber to the Court of Persia. Languishest thou not for my art? Lo! with three sweeps I’ll give thee a clean poll, all save the Identical! and I can discern and save it; fear me not, nor distrust my skill and the cunning that is mine.”
When he had heard Shibli Bagarag to a close, the countenance of Shagpat waxed fiery, as it had been flame kindled by travellers at night in a thorny bramble-bush, and he ruffled, and heaved, and was as when dense jungle-growths are stirred violently by the near approach of a wild animal in his fury, shouting in short breaths, “A barber! a barber! Is’t so? can it be? To me? A barber! O thou, thou reptile! filthy thing! A barber! O dog! A barber? What? when I bid fair for the highest honours known? O sacrilegious wretch! monster! How? are the afreets jealous, that they send thee to jibe me?”

===Chinese===

打定主意之后，他双眼始终死死盯着沙格帕特，腹中的饥火渐渐散去，化作一只饥肠辘辘的猛禽自他胸中振翅飞出，将沙格帕特定为捕食的猎物；再无任何力量能阻拦他，他非得迈步进店向沙格帕特陈情，试着将他剃光不可。
走入店铺御前，他高声致意道：“愿平安降临于你及你的亲族，衣袍商贾啊！”
沙格帕特答道：“愿平安亦降临于你！”
希布利·巴加拉格道：“我早已听闻你的赫赫大名，多毛的奇人啊！凭安拉起誓（Wullahy）！我今日特来向眼前所见之奇观顶礼膜拜。”
沙格帕特应道：“甚好！”
希布利·巴加拉格接着道：“盛赞我的机敏吧！我今日方才踏入贵城，便将进城的第一刀剃头盛举奉献于你，荣耀的纷繁卷发啊！”
闻听此言，沙格帕特面色一沉，粗声粗气道：“你的玩笑极其冒犯，陈腐不堪且缺乏神圣敬意，极不合时宜。”
希布利·巴加拉格却高呼道：“绝非玩笑，裁剪凡人体面外袍之人！实乃万分真切的盛情。”
沙格帕特的面孔顿时涨红如灌木丛中极红的浆果，怒喝道：“住口！休得胡言！否则定要激怒我的脾气，激怒那些生着不止两只眼睛的神灵。”
然而希布利·巴加拉格依旧不依不饶，他连连眨眼，手舞足蹈，指着自己的头顶喊道：“善于度量尺寸之人啊，切莫坠入错误的圈套；我乃是一名理发师，在这座城里乃是世所罕见的奇人，正是设拉子的希布利·巴加拉格！须知我是波斯宫廷首席理发师、威名远扬的巴巴·穆斯塔法的亲侄儿。难道你心中不渴盼我的绝技吗？瞧！不出三刀（three sweeps），我便能还你一颗干干净净的光头，独独留下那根独一神发（the Identical）！我能辨认并保全它；休要惧怕我，莫要怀疑我精湛的技艺与通天的机巧。”
听完希布利·巴加拉格这番话，沙格帕特的面孔瞬间如夜行旅人在荆棘丛中点燃的篝火般怒焰升腾，他浑身须发倒竖，胸膛剧烈起伏，宛如暴怒的猛兽逼近时密林草木猛烈晃动的骇人景象，气喘吁吁地厉声咆哮：“理发师！理发师！当真如此？岂有此理？竟敢冲着我来？理发师！你这爬虫！污秽之物！理发师！你这野狗！理发师？怎么？正当我即将问鼎世间最高无上荣耀之际？你这亵渎神圣的恶贼！妖孽！怎么？莫非是恶灵（afreets）心生嫉妒，特遣你来嘲弄我不成？”

===Original===

Thereupon he set up a cry for his wife, and that woman rushed to him from an inner room, and fell upon Shibli Bagarag, belabouring him.
So, when she was weary of this, she said, “O light of my eyes! O golden crop and adorable man! what hath he done to thee?”
Shagpat answered, “ ’Tis a barber! and he hath sworn to shave me, and leave me not save shorn!”
Hardly had Shagpat spoken this, when she became limp with the hearing of it. Then Shibli Bagarag slunk from the shop; but without the crowd had increased, seeing an altercation, and as he took to his heels they followed him, and there was uproar in the streets of the city and in the air above them, as of raging genii, he like a started quarry doubling this way and that, and at the corners of streets and open places, speeding on till there was no breath in his body, the cry still after him that he had bearded Shagpat. At last they came up with him, and belaboured him each and all; it was a storm of thwacks that fell on the back of Shibli Bagarag. When they had wearied themselves in this fashion, they took him as had he been a stray bundle or a damaged bale, and hurled him from the gates of the city into the wilderness once more.
Now, when he was alone, he staggered awhile and then flung himself to the earth, looking neither to the right nor to the left, nor above. All he could think was, “O accursed old woman!” and this he kept repeating to himself for solace; as the poet says:

> “ ’Tis sure the special privilege of hate,

To curse the authors of our evil state.”

===Chinese===

言罢，他扯开嗓子呼唤妻子，那妇人从内室疾步冲出，扑向希布利·巴加拉格劈头盖脸便是一顿痛打。
打得精疲力竭之后，妇人方才喘着粗气问道：“我眸中的明灯啊！黄金般的须发、令人顶礼膜拜的夫君！这厮究竟对你干了什么大逆不道之事？”
沙格帕特怒吼道：“他是理发师！他发誓要将我剃光，不剃尽绝不罢休！”
妇人一听此言，吓得浑身瘫软如泥。希布利·巴加拉格趁机溜出店铺；然而门外的人群因见争吵早已越聚越多，见他拔腿狂奔，众人当即在后穷追不舍，城中大街小巷乃至半空之中皆响起了震天喧嚣，宛若群魔暴怒咆哮，他如被惊起的猎物般东躲西藏，在街角与开阔广场四处逃窜，狂奔至肺腑断气，身后依然回荡着他胆敢冒犯太岁沙格帕特的震天怒吼。暴民终于将他追上，群起围攻拳打脚踢；无数乱杖如暴风雨般倾泻在希布利·巴加拉格的后背之上。待打得手软尽兴之后，众人像对待丢弃的破包袱或破损的货包一般，将他一把扔出城门，再次摔落在大漠荒野之中。
重归孤身一人的他踉跄了几步，随后一头栽倒在黄沙之上，不看左，不看右，亦不看苍穹。脑海中唯一剩下的念头便是：“该死的老婆子！”他翻来覆去反复咒骂着以此自慰，正如诗人所云：

> “仇恨独具之特权，
> 莫若咒骂祸之源。”

===Original===

As he was thus complaining, behold the very old woman before him! And she wheezed, and croaked, and coughed, and shook herself, and screwed her face into a pleasing pucker, and assumed womanish airs, and swayed herself, like as do the full moons of the harem when the eye of the master is upon them. Having made an end of these prettinesses, she said, in a tone of soft insinuation, “O youth, nephew of the barber, look upon me.”
Shibli Bagarag knew her voice, and he would not look, thinking, “Oh, what a dreadful old woman is this! just calling on her name in detestation maketh her present to us.” So the old woman, seeing him resolute to shun her, leaned to him, and put one hand to her dress, and squatted beside him, and said, “O youth, thou hast been thwacked!”
He groaned, lifting not his face, nor saying aught. Then said she, “Art thou truly in search of great things, O youth?”
Still he groaned, answering no syllable. And she continued, “ ’Tis surely in sweet friendliness I ask. Art thou not a fair youth, one to entice a damsel to perfect friendliness?”
Louder yet did he groan at her words, thinking, “A damsel, verily!” So the old woman said, “I wot thou art angry with me; but now look up, O nephew of the barber! no time for vexation. What says the poet?﻿—

> “ ‘Cares the warrior for his wounds

When the steed in battle bounds?’

“Moreover:

> “ ‘Let him who grasps the crown strip not for shame,

Lest he expose what gain’d it blow and maim!’

===Chinese===

正当他如此悲愤埋怨之际，看哪，那个老妪竟赫然立在他眼前！只见她喘着粗气，哑声咳嗽，浑身颤巍巍抖动，把那张褶子脸挤出一抹自以为讨喜的媚笑，端起一副女儿家的娇态，扭动着腰肢，恰若后宫中承蒙主人眷顾顾盼生姿的绝色明月。待她摆完这番娇媚姿态，以极尽温柔娇嗔的嗓音说道：“少年啊，理发师的侄儿，瞧瞧我吧。”
希布利·巴加拉格一听这嗓音便知是谁，根本不愿抬头，心想：“噢，这是何等阴魂不散的可怖老妪！单是在心底愤恨念及她的名号，她便立时现身眼前。”老妪见他决意不肯看她，便凑上前去，一手提了提衣角，蹲伏在他身旁，说道：“少年啊，你挨了痛打啦！”
他痛苦呻吟着，头也不抬，一声不吭。老妪接着道：“少年啊，你当真是在求索旷世大业吗？”
他依然唯有呻吟，半个字也不答。她继续道：“我好心好意相问。你难道不是个俊美少年，足以引得佳人萌生万般柔情吗？”
听到这话，他呻吟得越发响亮，暗忖：“佳人，亏她说得出口！”老妪道：“我知道你正生我的气；但且抬起头来吧，理发师的侄儿！如今可不是赌气懊恼的时候。正如诗人所云：

> ‘战马扬蹄战阵跃，
> 勇士岂顾皮肉创？’

“况且古人亦云：

> ‘凡欲夺冠登极者，莫因羞耻卸甲裳，
> 恐将创伤残破躯，显露人前惹耻伤！’

===Original===

“So be it with thee and thy thwacking, O foolish youth! Hide it from thyself, thou silly one! What! thou hast been thwacked, and refusest the fruit of it﻿—which is resoluteness, strength of mind, sternness in pursuit of the object!”
Then she softened her tone to persuasiveness, saying, “ ’Twas written I should be the head of thy fortune, O Shibli Bagarag! and thou’lt be enviable among men by my aid, so look upon me, and (for I know thee famished) thou shalt presently be supplied with viands and bright wines and sweetmeats, delicacies to cheer thee.”
Now, the promise of food and provision was powerful with Shibli Bagarag, and he looked up gloomily. And the old woman smiled archly at him, and wriggled in her seat like a dusty worm, and said, “Dost thou find me charming, thou fair youth?”
He was nigh laughing in her face, but restrained himself to reply, “Thou art that thou art!”
Said she, “Not so, but that I shall be.” Then she said, “O youth, pay me now a compliment!”
Shibli Bagarag was at a loss what further to say to the old woman, for his heart cursed her for her persecutions, and ridiculed her for her vanities. At last he bethought himself of the saying of the poet, truly the offspring of fine wit, where he says:

> “Expect no flatteries from me,

While I am empty of good things;

I’ll call thee fair, and I’ll agree

Thou boldest Love in silken strings,

When thou hast primed me from thy plenteous store!

But, oh! till then a clod am I:

No seed within to throw up flowers:

All’s drouthy to the fountain dry:

To empty stomachs Nature lowers:

The lake was full where heaven look’d fair of yore!”

===Chinese===

“你挨的这顿乱杖亦是同理，愚笨的少年啊！休要将它挂在心头，痴顽之人！怎么！你挨了一顿皮肉痛打，难道竟要拒绝它结出的善果——那便是坚韧的心志、刚毅的魄力，以及誓达目的的决绝之勇！”
随后她将语调放得极尽温存诱惑，劝道：“宿命早有注定，我将成为你飞黄腾达的引路源头，希布利·巴加拉格！得我相助，你必成为天下男儿钦羡的大英雄；所以抬起头来看看我吧，（我深知你已饥肠辘辘）我立刻便为你奉上美馔佳肴、清澈美酒与精美蜜饯，用各色美味抚慰你的饥肠。”
美酒佳肴的许诺对希布利·巴加拉格而言具有无法抗拒的魔力，他神色阴郁地抬起了头。老妪朝他俏皮一笑，在地上扭动着身躯宛若一条满身泥尘的蚯蚓，娇声问道：“俊俏的少年郎，你觉得我可算迷人动人？”
他险些当场笑出声来，但勉强按捺住自己答道：“你本是何模样，便是何模样！”
她道：“非也，我乃是我未来之模样。”接着她又道：“少年啊，快赞美我两句吧！”
希布利·巴加拉格一时语塞，不知该如何应对这老妪，皆因他心底既怨恨她的连番捉弄，又鄙夷她的自作多情与虚荣。终于，他忆起了诗人巧夺天工的传世名句，诗云：

> “休指望我口吐逢迎，
> 当我腹中空空如也；
> 待你以丰盛珍馐填饱我肠，
> 我自赞你貌若天仙，
> 叹你以柔情丝绦执掌爱火！
> 噢！在此之前我不过是一块顽石：
> 体内并无破土绽花的生机；
> 泉眼干涸之处万物焦渴：
> 饥肠辘辘前大自然亦为之垂首：
> 唯有蓄满春水之湖，昔日方倒映晴空万里！”

===Original===

So, when he had spoken that, the old woman laughed and exclaimed, “Thou art apt! it is well said! Surely I excuse thee till that time! Now listen! ’Tis written we work together, and I know it by divination. Have I not known thee wandering, and on thy way to this city of Shagpat, where thou’lt some day sit throned? Now I propose to thee this﻿—and ’tis an excellent proposal﻿—that I lead thee to great things, and make thee glorious, a sitter in high seats, Master of an Event?”
Cried he, “A proposal honourable to thee, and pleasant in the ear.”
She added, “Provided thou marry me in sweet marriage.”
Thereat he stared on vacancy with a serious eye, and he could scarce credit her earnestness, but she repeated the same. So presently he thought, “This old hag appeareth deep in the fountain of events, and she will be a right arm to me in the mastering of one, a torch in darkness, seeing there is wisdom in her as well as wickedness. The thwackings?﻿—sad was their taste, but they’re in the road leading to greatness, and I cannot say she put me out of that road in putting me where they were. Her age?﻿—shall I complain of that when it is a sign she goeth shortly altogether?”
As he was thus debating he regarded the old woman stealthily, and she was in agitation, so that her joints creaked like forest branches in a wind, and the puckers of her visage moved as do billows of the sea to and fro, and the anticipations of a fair young bride are not more eager than what was visible in the old woman. Wheedlingly she looked at him, and shaped her mouth like a bird’s bill to soften it; and she drew together her dress, to give herself the look of slimness, using all fascinations. He thought, “ ’Tis a wondrous old woman! Marriage would seem a thing of moment to her, yet is the profit with me, and I’ll agree to it.” So he said, “ ’Tis a pact between us, O old woman!”
Now, the eyes of the old woman brightened when she heard him, and were as the eyes of a falcon that eyeth game, hungry with red fire, and she looked brisk with impatience, laughing a low laugh and saying, “O youth, I must claim of thee, as is usual in such cases, the kiss of contract.”
So Shibli Bagarag was mindful of what is written,

> “If thou wouldst take the great leap, be ready for the little jump,”

===Chinese===

听完这番诗句，老妪放声大笑高呼道：“你委实机敏！说得好极！在饱餐之前我权且宽宥你！如今且听好！冥冥中自有天数注定你我共谋大业，我凭占卜洞悉无遗。我岂会不知你正四处漂泊，正前往这座沙格帕特城，而有朝一日你将在此君临天下、端坐宝座？如今我向你提出这一提议——端的是绝妙之策——由我引你成就旷世伟业，教你荣光万丈，高居庙堂之巅，成为命定大事的主宰（Master of an Event）？”
他高呼道：“这提议于你体面，于我听来亦极其悦耳。”
她补充道：“前提是你须与我结为连理，共缔良缘。”
听到这里，希布利·巴加拉格神色肃然，呆呆凝视着虚空，几乎不敢相信她是认真的，可老妪言之凿凿再次重申。不多时他暗忖：“这老太婆似乎深谙天机大势，若要降伏宿命，她定能成为我的左膀右臂，成为黑暗中的指路明灯，毕竟她既有机巧亦兼具智慧。至于挨的那两顿乱杖？——滋味固然凄惨，但这本就是通往伟业的必由之路，也不能全怪她将我引到了挨打之地。至于她的年岁？——她这把风烛残年的年纪，岂不正预示着她不久便要归天了吗，我又有何可抱怨的？”
正当他心中反复盘算之际，他偷偷打量了老妪一眼，只见她激动得浑身发抖，关节如狂风中的枯木树枝般嘎吱作响，脸上的层层褶皱如海浪般此起彼伏，一个如花似玉的年轻新娘对大婚的期盼，也绝不会比这老妪流露出的狂热更为急切。她媚态横生凝望着他，噘起嘴唇如鸟喙般装出娇柔温存之态；紧紧收拢衣衫以显出身段的窈窕，用尽了百般风情。他暗想：“这真是一个奇绝的老婆子！成亲在她眼中似乎重如泰山，可得利的分明是我，我便应允了吧。”于是他道：“一言为定，老婆子！”
听闻此言，老妪双眸顿时神采大放，宛若盯上猎物的猎鹰之眸，燃烧着贪婪的赤红欲火；她显得迫不及待、急切万分，低笑一声说道：“少年啊，按凡间常礼，我须向你讨要一记定亲之吻。”
希布利·巴加拉格深知古训所言：

> “若欲纵身跨万丈，
> 且须先做小跳跃，”

===Original===

and he stretched out his mouth to the forehead of the old woman. When he had done so, it was as though she had been illuminated, as when light is put in the hollow of a pumpkin. Then said she, “This is well! this is a fair beginning! Now look, for thy fortune will of a surety follow. Call me now sweet bride, and knocker at the threshold of hearts!”
So Shibli Bagarag sighed, and called her this, and he said, “Forget not my condition, O old woman, and that I am nigh famished.”
Upon that she nodded gravely, and arose and shook her garments together, and beckoned for Shibli Bagarag to follow her; and the two passed through the gates of the city, and held on together through divers streets and thoroughfares till they came before the doors of a palace with a pillared entrance; and the old woman passed through the doors of the palace as one familiar to them, and lo! they were in a lofty court, built all of marble, and in the middle of it a fountain playing, splashing silvery. Shibli Bagarag would have halted here to breathe the cool refreshingness of the air, but the old woman would not; and she hurried on even to the opening of a spacious hall, and in it slaves in circle round a raised seat, where sat one that was their lord, and it was the Chief Vizier of the King.
Then the old woman turned round sharply to Shibli Bagarag, and said, “How of thy tackle, O my betrothed?”
He answered, “The edge is keen, the hand ready.”
Then said she, “ ’Tis well.”
So the old woman put her two hands on the shoulders of Shibli Bagarag, saying, “Make thy reverence to him on the raised seat; have faith in thy tackle and in me. Renounce not either, whatsoever ensueth. Be not abashed, O my bridegroom to be!”
Thereupon she thrust him in; and Shibli Bagarag was abashed, and played foolishly with his fingers, knowing not what to do. So when the Chief Vizier saw him he cried out, “Who art thou, and what wantest thou?”
Now, the back of Shibli Bagarag tingled when he heard the Vizier’s voice, and he said, “I am, O man of exalted condition, he whom men know as Shibli Bagarag, nephew to Baba Mustapha, the renowned of Shiraz; myself barber likewise, proud of my art, prepared to exercise it.”
Then said the Chief Vizier, “This even to our faces! Wonderful is the audacity of impudence! Know, O nephew of the barber, thou art among them that honour not thy art.

===Chinese===

于是他凑过嘴去，在老妪的额头上吻了一记。就在他吻下的一刹那，老妪周身仿佛被点亮了一般，宛如在掏空的南瓜腹中点燃了一盏明灯。接着她喜道：“甚好！这当真是绝妙的开端！且瞧着吧，你的大运必将接踵而至。如今且尊称我一声娇俏的新娘、扣响心扉之人吧！”
希布利·巴加拉格不由长叹一声，依言如此称呼了她，随后道：“老婆子，可切莫忘了我的处境，我如今可是饿得前胸贴后背了。”
老妪庄重地点了点头，翻身站起，抖了抖身上的衣衫，向希布利·巴加拉格招手示意随她前行；二人穿过城门，并肩穿行过一条条纵横交错的街巷大道，直至来到一座立柱门楼的巍峨王府门前；老妪轻车熟路迈入王府大门，瞧啊！他们置身于一座全由纯白大理石筑起的巍峨庭院之中，庭院正中央的一座喷泉正喷涌着银花碎玉。希布利·巴加拉格本欲停下脚步深吸一口清凉宜人的空气，老妪却不容他停留；她一路催促疾行，径直来到一座宽敞大殿的门前，只见殿内奴仆成群，团团环侍在一座高台宝座周围，宝座上端坐着他们的主人，正是国王的首席宰相（Chief Vizier）。
老妪猛然转过身来对着希布利·巴加拉格说道：“你的理发器械如今可还趁手，我的未婚夫？”
他答道：“锋刃极其锋利，双手早已备好。”
她道：“甚好。”
老妪将双手搭在希布利·巴加拉格的肩头，叮嘱道：“向高台上的大人行礼；坚信你的器械，坚信于我。无论接踵而至发生何事，绝不可放弃其中任何一样。切莫惊慌失措，我未来的夫君啊！”
言罢，她猛力将他推入殿中；希布利·巴加拉格羞怯窘迫，两手局促不安地搓弄着手指，不知所措。首席宰相一见他，便厉声喝问：“你是何人，有何企图？”
希布利·巴加拉格一听这声音，后背顿时火辣辣地发麻，他答道：“尊贵的大人，在下便是人称希布利·巴加拉格之人，乃是设拉子大名鼎鼎的巴巴·穆斯塔法的侄儿；我自己亦是一名理发师，深以为手艺自豪，时刻准备施展技艺。”
首席宰相怒道：“竟敢当着本官的面口出狂言！当真狂妄嚣张至极！理发师的侄儿，可知你如今置身于视你的手艺如粪土之人面前。

===Original===

Is it not written, ‘For one thing thou shalt be crowned here, for that thing be thwacked there?’ So also it is written, ‘The tongue of the insolent one is a lash and a perpetual castigation to him.’ And it is written, O Shibli Bagarag, that I reap honour from thee, and there is no help but that thou be made an example of.”
So the Chief Vizier uttered command, and Shibli Bagarag was ware of the power of five slaves upon him; and they seized him familiarly, and placed him in position, and made ready his clothing for the reception of fifty other thwacks with a thong, each several thwack coming down on him with a hiss, as it were a serpent, and with a smack, as it were the mouth of satisfaction; and the people assembled extolled the Chief Vizier, saying, “Well and valiantly done, O stay of the State! and suchlike to the accursed race of barbers.”
Now, when they had passed before the Chief Vizier and departed, lo! he fell to laughing violently, so that his hair was agitated and was as a sand-cloud over him, and his countenance behind it was as the sun of the desert reflected ripplingly on the waters of a bubbling spring, for it had the aspect of merriness; and the Chief Vizier exclaimed, “O Shibli Bagarag, have I not made fair show?”
And Shibli Bagarag said, “Excellent fair show, O mighty one!” Yet knew he not in what, but he was abject by reason of the thwacks.
So the Vizier said, “Thou lookest lean, even as one to whom Fortune oweth a long debt. Tell me now of thy barbercraft: perchance thy gain will be great thereby?”
And Shibli Bagarag answered, “My gain has been great, O eminent in rank, but of evil quality, and I am content not to increase it.” And he broke forth into lamentations, crying in excellent verse:﻿—

> “Why am I thus the sport of all﻿—

A thing Fate knocketh like a ball

From point to point of evil chance,

Even as the sneer of Circumstance?

While thirsting for the highest fame,

I hunger like the lowest beast:

To be the first of men I aim

And find myself the least.”

===Chinese===

“经文上难道未曾写明：‘在此地教你加冕受尊之事，在彼处必教你饱尝乱杖鞭笞’？经书亦云：‘狂悖狂徒之长舌，乃其招致鞭挞笞楚之根由。’经文更曾注定，希布利·巴加拉格啊，我当从你身上获取功勋荣光，如今非拿你杀一儆百以儆效尤不可。”
首席宰相一声令下，希布利·巴加拉格再度领教了五名奴仆（five slaves）的通天蛮力；他们熟门熟路将他一把按倒在地，剥开他的衣衫，准备让他再领教另外五十下皮鞭（fifty other thwacks with a thong）；每一鞭抽落下来皆带着毒蛇吐信般的凄厉呼啸，伴随着大功告成般的脆响；聚拢在大殿周遭的众人齐声盛赞首席宰相，高呼道：“大人打得好！真乃社稷砥柱！对待该死的理发师一脉就该如此严惩不贷。”
待群仆在首席宰相御前退散离去之后，看哪！宰相突然放声狂笑起来，直笑得须发剧烈抖动，如一片沙尘暴将他笼罩，在狂乱须发后方的那张面孔，恰似映照在汩汩泉水倒影中的大漠旭日，洋溢着难以自禁的欢快；首席宰相高声喊道：“希布利·巴加拉格，我方才这出戏演得如何？”
希布利·巴加拉格答道：“大人演得惟妙惟肖，威武非凡！”可他浑然不知妙在何处，只觉得挨了这顿痛打后整个人低贱到了尘埃里。
宰相说道：“你瞧来面黄肌瘦，倒像个时运欠他一笔巨债的倒霉鬼。如今同我细细讲讲你的理发绝艺吧：兴许借此你能发一笔大财呢？”
希布利·巴加拉格答道：“位高权重的大人啊，我得的‘进益’早已满坑满谷，只可惜全是皮肉受苦的晦气进益，我可不敢再多求半点了。”说罢他捶胸顿足，以绝妙的诗句悲叹道：

> “我何以沦为万众笑柄——
> 命运将我当球肆意踢打，
> 在厄运深渊中滚落辗转，
> 沦为世道讥诮的玩物？
> 渴盼名扬天下之日，
> 饥肠辘辘如低贱牲畜：
> 志在问鼎人上之人，
> 暮然回首身卑如泥。”

===Original===

Now, the Vizier delayed not when he heard this to have a fair supply set before Shibli Bagarag, and meats dressed in divers fashions, spiced, and coloured, and with herbs, and wines in golden goblets, and slaves in attendance. So Shibli Bagarag ate and drank, and presently his soul arose from its prostration, and he cried, “*Wullahy!* the head cook of King Shamshureen could have worked no better as regards the restorative process.”
Then said the Chief Vizier, “O Shibli Bagarag, where now is thy tackle?”
And Shibli Bagarag winked and nodded and turned his head in the manner of the knowing ones, and he recited the verse:

> “ ’Tis well that we are sometimes circumspect,

And hold ourselves in witless ways deterred:

One thwacking made me seriously reflect;

A *second* turned the cream of love to curd:

Most surely that profession I reject

Before the fear of a prospective *third*.”

So the Vizier said, “ ’Tis well, thou turnest verse neatly.” And he exclaimed extemporaneously:

> “If thou wouldst have thy achievement as high

As the wings of Ambition can fly:

If thou the clear summit of hope wouldst attain,

And not have thy labour in vain;

Be steadfast in that which impell’d, for the peace

Of earth he who leaves must have trust:

He is safe while he soars, but when faith shall cease,

Desponding he drops to the dust.”

Then said he, “Fear no further thwacking, but honour and prosperity in the place of it. What says the poet?﻿—

> “ ‘We faint, when for the fire

There needs one spark;

We droop, when our desire

Is near its mark.’

===Chinese===

宰相闻听此言，毫不迟疑，立时命人备下一桌丰盛至极的筵席呈在希布利·巴加拉格面前，各色烹调考究的肉食，拌着香料、缀着鲜艳佐料与香草，金樽中注满了美酒，奴仆在侧侍奉。希布利·巴加拉格大快朵颐、痛饮美酒，不多时神魂便自颓丧中重新振作，他高呼道：“凭安拉起誓（Wullahy）！论起起死回生抚慰肚肠的手段，纵是沙姆舒林国王（King Shamshureen）的御厨总管亦莫过于此了。”
首席宰相随即问道：“希布利·巴加拉格，你的理发器械如今安在？”
希布利·巴加拉格连连眨眼点头，学着聪明人的模样摇晃着脑门，吟诵诗句道：

> “世人偶怀审慎心，实乃明智之举，
> 免教自身在愚妄歧途上越陷越深：
> 一遭痛打教我闭门苦苦思过；
> 二遭毒打将恩爱情分化作齑粉：
> 面对恐将临头的第三遭痛打，
> 我定当将这理发行当弃若敝屣。”

宰相笑道：“好极，你的诗韵编排得颇为精巧。”随后他即兴赋诗道：

> “若欲功成凌绝顶，
> 齐肩雄心展翅飞；
> 若欲登临希望峰，
> 不教平生心血亏；
> 当怀初心志莫移，
> 弃绝安逸须信纯：
> 展翅翱翔保平安，
> 信念若熄堕红尘。”

随后宰相道：“休要再惧怕任何皮鞭之苦，取而代之的必将是无上荣光与富贵。正如诗人所云：

> ‘当烈火仅需一点火星，
> 人往往心力交瘁；
> 当宏愿已近在咫尺，
> 人往往意兴阑珊。’

===Original===

“How near to it art thou, O Shibli Bagarag! Know, then, that among this people there is great reverence for the growing of hair, and he that is hairiest is honoured most, wherefore are barbers creatures of especial abhorrence, and of a surety flourish not. And so it is that I owe my station to the esteem I profess for the cultivation of hair, and to my persecution of the clippers of it. And in this kingdom is no one that beareth such a crop as I, saving one, a clothier, an accursed one!﻿—and may a blight fall upon him for his vanity and his affectation of solemn priestliness, and his lolling in his shopfront to be admired and marvelled at by the people. So this fellow I would disgrace and bring to scorn﻿—this Shagpat! for he is mine enemy, and the eye of the King my master is on him. Now I conceive thy assistance in this matter, Shibli Bagarag﻿—thou, a barber.”
When Shibli Bagarag heard mention of Shagpat, and the desire for vengeance in the Vizier, he was as a new man, and he smelt the sweetness of his own revenge as a vulture smelleth the carrion from afar, and he said, “I am thy servant, thy slave, O Vizier!” Then smiled he as to his own soul, and he exclaimed, “On my head be it!”
And it was to him as when sudden gusts of perfume from garden roses of the valley meet the traveller’s nostril on the hill that overlooketh the valley, filling him with ecstasy and newness of life, delicate visions. And he cried, “*Wullahy!* this is fair; this is well! I am he that was appointed to do thy work, O man in office! What says the poet?﻿—

> “ ‘The destined hand doth strike the fated blow:

Surely the arrow’s fitted to the bow!’

“And he says:

> “ ‘The feathered seed for the wind delayeth,

The wind above the garden swayeth,

The garden of its burden knoweth,

The burden falleth, sinketh, soweth.’ ”

So the Vizier chuckled and nodded, saying, “Right, right! aptly spoken, O youth of favour! ’Tis even so, and there is wisdom in what is written:

> “ ‘Chance is a poor knave;

Its own sad slave;

Two meet that were to meet:

Life’s no cheat.’ ”

===Chinese===

“你离那大业已是何其之近啊，希布利·巴加拉格！须知此地百姓对蓄发生长怀有极大的崇敬，谁的发量最盛，谁便得享至高尊崇，正因如此，理发师成了人见人嫌的厌物，绝无容身之地。而我之所以能官居宰相高位，全赖我对蓄发风尚的狂热推崇，以及对剪须剃发之流的残酷迫害。在这座王国之中，除却一人之外，再无人能蓄出如我这般繁茂的长发，那人乃是个布商，一个该死的恶棍！——愿天降灾祸严惩他的虚荣自负与伪装圣者的道貌岸然，以及整日仰卧在自家铺前诱引百姓顶礼膜拜的无耻行径。我定要让这厮身败名裂、沦为万人笑柄——那厮正是沙格帕特！他乃是我的宿敌，而我的主子国王如今已对他青眼有加。在这桩大事上，希布利·巴加拉格，我正需依仗你的鼎力相助——凭你理发师的身份。”
希布利·巴加拉格一听提及沙格帕特之名，得知宰相复仇的宏愿，整个人顿时精神焕发宛若新生，他嗅到了属于自己复仇的甘美滋味，恰似秃鹫自千里之外嗅到了腐肉的腥甜，他说道：“在下愿效犬马之劳，全凭宰相大人差遣！”随后他心花怒放，暗自窃喜，高声宣告：“凡事皆包在我身上！”
此时此刻，他只觉得仿佛旅人伫立在俯瞰山谷的高崖之上，山谷花园中突如其来的一阵玫瑰幽香扑鼻而来，令他神魂颠倒、重获新生，眼前浮现出无限美妙的幻象。他高呼道：“凭安拉起誓（Wullahy）！妙哉，善哉！我正是命中注定替大人成就伟业之人，当权的大人啊！正如诗人所言：

> ‘宿命之手必挥命定之击：
> 离弦之箭早已搭上坚弓！’

“诗人亦云：

> ‘带羽之种静候清风起，
> 狂风徐徐拂过花园际，
> 花园明晓自身之所承，
> 重负落定破土根芽生。’”

宰相咯咯轻笑连连点头，道：“不错，不错！讲得极妙，深得我心的青年啊！事实正是如此，古书所载自有一番大智慧：

> ‘机运本是可怜汉；
> 自身悲苦为奴绊；
> 注定相逢终聚首：
> 人生绝无虚妄诞。’”

===Original===

Upon that he cried, “First let us have with us the Eclipser of Reason, and take counsel with her, as is my custom.”
Now, the Vizier made signal to a slave in attendance, and the slave departed from the hall, and the Vizier led Shibli Bagarag into a closer chamber, which had a smooth floor of inlaid silver and silken hangings, the windows looking forth on the gardens of the palace and its fountains and cool recesses of shade and temperate sweetness. While they sat there conversing in this metre and that, measuring quotations, lo! the old woman, the affianced of Shibli Bagarag﻿—and she sumptuously arrayed, in perfect queenliness, her head bound in a circlet of gems and gold, her figure lustrous with a full robe of flowing crimson silk; and she wore slippers embroidered with golden traceries, and round her waist a girdle flashing with jewels, so that to look on she was as a long falling water in the last bright slant of the sun. Her hair hung disarranged, and spread in a scattered fashion off her shoulders; and she was younger by many moons, her brow smooth where Shibli Bagarag had given the kiss of contract, her hand soft and white where he had taken it. Shibli Bagarag was smitten with astonishment at sight of her, and he thought, “Surely the aspect of this old woman would realise the story of Bhanavar the Beautiful; and it is a story marvellous to think of; yet how great is the likeness between Bhanavar and this old woman that groweth younger!”
And he thought again, “What if the story of Bhanavar be a true one; this old woman such as she﻿—no other?”
So, while he considered her, the Vizier exclaimed, “Is she not fair﻿—my daughter?”
And the youth answered, “She is, O Vizier, that she is!”
But the Vizier cried, “Nay, by Allah! she is that she will be.” And the Vizier said, “ ’Tis she that is my daughter; tell me thy thought of her, as thou thinkest it.”
And Shibli Bagarag replied, “O Vizier, my thought of her is, she seemeth indeed as Bhanavar the Beautiful﻿—no other.”
Then the Vizier and the Eclipser of Reason exclaimed together, “How of Bhanavar and her story, O youth? We listen!”
So Shibli Bagarag leaned slightly on a cushion of a couch, and narrated as followeth.

===Chinese===

随后宰相高声唤道：“且让我们召来倾国倾城之女（the Eclipser of Reason），按我的惯例同她从长计议。”
宰相向随侍的奴仆打了个手势，奴仆领命退下大殿，宰相引着希布利·巴加拉格步入一间更为幽静的内室，室内铺着嵌银的平滑地板，悬挂着道道丝帷，窗外正对着王府的园林、喷泉以及清凉怡人的林荫幽境。正当二人对坐畅谈、以各种诗韵切磋酬唱之际，看哪！希布利·巴加拉格订亲的老妪步入室中——她身披极其奢华的盛装，具足女王般的威仪，头上戴着金丝缠绕宝石的头冠，身着一袭泛着流光溢彩的猩红丝绸长袍；脚踏绣着金纹的绣履，腰间系着一条镶嵌各色闪烁宝石的玉带，望之恰若落日斜阳最后一抹余晖下倾泻而下的长长瀑布。她的秀发略显凌乱，随意披散在香肩两侧；整个人比先前年轻了许多岁月，希布利·巴加拉格落下定亲之吻的额头平滑无皱，他执过的手掌亦雪白娇嫩。希布利·巴加拉格目睹此景惊骇万分，暗想：“这老妪的模样真能印证绝代佳人巴纳瓦尔（Bhanavar the Beautiful）的传奇故事；回想起来那当真是一段匪夷所思的传说；可巴纳瓦尔与眼前这越活越年轻的老妇人何其相似！”
他又暗忖：“倘若巴纳瓦尔的故事千真万确，眼前这老妪便是她本人，绝无二致呢？”
正当他反复端详之际，宰相高声问道：“令媛生得美貌吗？”
青年答道：“回禀大人，她本是何模样，便是何模样！”
宰相却大笑道：“非也，指着安拉起誓！她乃是她未来之模样。”接着宰相道：“她便是在下的女儿；如实告知我你对她的评价。”
希布利·巴加拉格答道：“大人，在下以为，她委实与绝代佳人巴纳瓦尔毫无二致。”
宰相与倾国倾城之女异口同声惊呼道：“巴纳瓦尔与她的传奇究竟是何因由，少年啊？我们洗耳恭听！”
于是希布利·巴加拉格斜倚在软榻的靠垫上，娓娓道来下文所述的传奇。

## The Veiled Figure / 蒙面人
===Original===

Verily there was lightning in Aklis as Shibli Bagarag flashed the Sword over the clamouring beasts: the shape of the great palace stood forth vividly, and a wide illumination struck up the streams, and gilded the large hanging leaves, and drew the hills glimmeringly together, and scattered fires on the flat faces of the rocks. Then the seven youths said quickly, “Away! out of Aklis, O Master of the Event! from city to city of Earth this light is visible, and men will know that Fate is in travail, and an Event preparing for them, and Shagpat will be warned by the portent; wherefore lose not the happy point of time on which thy star is manifest.” And they cried again, “Away! out of Aklis!” with gestures of impatience, urging his departure.
Then said he, “O youths, Sons of Aklis, it is written that gratitude is the poor man’s mine of wealth, and the rich man’s flower of beauty; and I have but that to give ye for all this aid and friendliness of yours.”
But they exclaimed, “No aid or friendliness in Aklis! By the gall of the Roc! it is well for thee thou camest armed with potent spells, and hadst one to advise and inspirit thee, or thou wouldst have stayed here to people Aklis, and grazed in a strange shape.”
Now, the seven waxed in impatience, and he laid their hands upon his head and moved from them with Abarak, to where in the dusk the elephant that had brought them stood. Then the elephant kneeled and took the twain upon his back, and bore them across the dark land to that reach of the river where the boat was moored in readiness. They entered the boat silently among its drapery of lotuses, and the Veiled Figure ferried them over the stream that rippled not with their motion. As they were crossing, desire to know that Veiled Figure counselled Shibli Bagarag evilly to draw the Sword again, and flash it, so that the veil became transparent. Then, when Abarak turned to him for the reason of the flashing of the Sword, he beheld the eyes of the youth fixed in horror, glaring as at sights beyond the tomb.

===Chinese===

委实如此，当希布利·巴加拉格（Shibli Bagarag）在那群喧嚣咆哮的禽兽头顶挥动宝剑之时，阿克利斯（Aklis）境内闪现出道道雷霆电光：巍峨的宏伟宫殿赫然耸立显形，大片光华掠过溪流，为低垂舒展的阔叶镀上一层金辉，令群山在幽微闪烁中聚拢相连，更将如火烈焰洒向一块块平整如砥的岩石。随即，那七名青年急促地喊道：“快走！速速离开阿克利斯，命定之人啊！这道光芒已照彻人间的座座城邑，世人皆已知晓命运正在孕育翻天巨变，大事将临；而沙格帕特（Shagpat）亦必被这异象所惊觉。因此万勿错失你命星显照的吉祥良机！”他们做出急不可耐的手势，一再催促他启程，齐声高呼：“快走！速速离开阿克利斯！”
希布利·巴加拉格于是道：“阿克利斯之子（Sons of Aklis）啊，青年们，古训有云：感恩乃穷人的无尽宝藏，亦是富人的娇艳繁花；面对你们这般倾力相助与善意，在下唯有以这满腔感激相报。”
不料他们却嚷道：“阿克利斯哪有什么相助与善意！凭大鹏鸟（Roc）的苦胆起誓！全仗着你来时身怀无上法咒，又有人从旁提点、为你振作精神，否则你早就留在这儿充实阿克利斯的人口，化作异类禽兽低头吃草去啦。”
此时，这七人愈发显得焦躁不安。青年将他们的双手按在自己头上以示敬意，便同阿巴拉克（Abarak）一道离开他们，走向暮色中先前载他们前来的那头巨象驻足之处。巨象跪下身子，将二人驮在背上，载着他们穿过昏暗幽深的原野，直抵那段早已系泊着小舟的河湾。他们悄然步入掩映在垂垂莲花丛中的小舟，那位蒙面人（Veiled Figure）便摇橹摆渡，载着二人渡过那水面甚至不起一丝波澜的河流。行渡之际，急欲探究蒙面人真面目的念头诱使希布利·巴加拉格行了不义之举——他再度拔出宝剑，让剑刃金光一闪，那面纱瞬间化作半透明。正当阿巴拉克扭过头来想问明他为何让宝剑发光时，只见那青年的双眼满含惊骇、直勾勾凝固在前方，宛如窥见了幽冥坟冢之外的骇人景象。

===Original===

He said nought, but as the boat’s-head whispered among the reeds and long flowers of the opposite marge, he took Shibli Bagarag by the shoulders and pushed him out of the boat, and leaped out likewise, leading him from the marge forcibly, hurrying him forward from it, he at the heels of the youth propelling him; and crying in out-of-breath voice at intervals, “What sight? what sight?” But the youth was powerless of speech, and when at last he opened his lips, the little man shrank from him, for he laughed as do the insane, a peal of laughter ended by gasps; then a louder peal, presently softer; then a peal that started all the echoes in Aklis. After awhile, as Abarak still cried in his ear, “What sight?” he looked at him with a large eye, saying querulously, “Is it written I shall be pushed by the shoulder through life? And is it in the pursuit of further thwackings?”
Abarak heeded him not, crying still, “What sight?” and Shibli Bagarag lowered his tone, and jerked his body, pronouncing the name “Rabesqurat!” Then Abarak exclaimed, “ ’Tis as I weened. Oh, fool! to flash the Sword and peer through the veil! Truly, there be few wits will bear that sight!” On a sudden he cried, “No cure but one, and that a sleep in the bosom of the betrothed!”
Thereupon he hurried the youth yet faster across the dark lawns of Aklis toward the passage of the Seventh Pillar, by which the twain had entered that kingdom. And Shibli Bagarag saw as in a dream the shattered door, shattered by the bar, remembering dimly as a thing distant in years the netting of the Queen, and Noorna chained upon the pillar; he remembered Shagpat even vacantly in his mind, as one sheaf of barley amid other sheaves of the bearded field, so was he overcome by the awfulness of that sight behind the veil of the Veiled Figure!
As they advanced to the passage, he was aware of an impediment to its entrance, as it had been a wall of stone there; and seeing Abarak enter the passage without let, he kicked hard in front at the invisible obstruction, but there was no coming by. Abarak returned to him, and took his right arm, and raised the sleeve from his wrist, and lo, the two remaining hairs of Garraveen twisted round it in sapphire winds. Cried he, “Oh, the generosity of Gulrevaz! she has left these two hairs that he may accomplish swiftly the destiny marked for him!

===Chinese===

阿巴拉克一言不发，但当舟头轻柔滑过对岸的芦苇与修长花丛、发出窸窣细语时，他一把攥住希布利·巴加拉格的肩头，将他推出小舟，自己也跟着一跃上岸。他强拽着青年快步远离河岸，紧随在青年脚后推着他向前疾走，中途还上气不接下气地连连追问：“你见着什么了？到底见着什么了？”然而青年舌僵语塞，当他终于张开嘴唇时，矮小汉子却被吓得往后一缩——因为青年竟像疯子般狂笑起来，一阵阵笑声最后化为急促的喘息；紧接着是一阵更响亮的狂笑，旋即又转为轻微的抽泣冷笑；随即爆发出的一阵大笑更是震得整个阿克利斯回声四起。过了好一会儿，阿巴拉克仍在他耳边急声追问“你见着什么了？”，青年圆睁着一双空洞的大眼看着他，哀怨地嘟囔道：“难道命中注定我这一生都要被人推着肩膀往前走吗？难道还要接着去挨更多板子不成？”
阿巴拉克根本不理会他的胡话，依旧连声逼问：“见着什么了？”希布利·巴加拉格这才压低声音，身子冷不丁抽搐了一下，吐出了一个名字：“拉贝斯库拉特（Rabesqurat）！”阿巴拉克顿时惊呼道：“果然不出我所料。噢，你这蠢材！竟敢挥剑照亮面纱去偷看！当真没几个凡人的神智受得住那等景象！”蓦地他又大叫道：“普天之下别无解法，唯有一途——那就是在他未婚妻的怀中沉沉睡上一觉！”
说罢，他催促着青年以更快的脚步穿过阿克利斯昏暗的草坪，奔向第七根石柱（Seventh Pillar）的通道，二人当初正是由此进入这座王国的。希布利·巴加拉格恍若身在梦境，隐隐约约看见那扇被铁闩砸得粉碎的门户，恍如隔世般依稀记起女王被网罗擒获、努尔娜被锁在石柱上的往事；他脑海中甚至只能模模糊糊想起沙格帕特，就如同在长满麦芒的田野里想起其中某一捆大麦一般——那蒙面人面纱背后的骇人惨象，竟将他的心神彻底击垮震慑至此！
行至通道跟前，希布利·巴加拉格察觉到入口处似有一道无形的屏障，坚硬宛如一堵石墙；眼见阿巴拉克畅通无阻地步入通道，他拼尽全力朝面前看不见的阻碍狠命踹去，却无论如何也跨不过去。阿巴拉克折返回来，抓起他的右手，将衣袖从手腕处向上挽起，看哪！加拉文（Garraveen）仅剩的两根马鬃正化作蓝宝石般的微风缠绕在他的腕间。阿巴拉克叫道：“噢，古尔雷瓦兹（Gulrevaz）当真仁慈慷慨！她留下这两根马鬃，好让他能迅速完成为他注定的天命！

===Original===

but now, since his gazing through that veil, he must part with them to get out of Aklis.” And he muttered, “His star is a strange one! one that leadeth him to fortune by the path of frowns! to greatness by the aid of thwackings! Truly the ways of Allah are wonderful!” Shibli Bagarag resisted him in nothing, and Abarak loosed the two bright hairs from his wrist, and those two hairs swelled and took glittering scales, and were sapphire snakes with wings of intense emerald; and they rose in the air spirally together, each over each, so that to see them one would fancy in the darkness a fountain of sapphire waters flashed with the sheen of emerald. When they had reached a height loftier than the topmost palace-towers of Aklis, they descended like javelins into the earth, and in a moment reappeared, in the shape of genii when they are charitably disposed to them they visit; not much above the mortal size, nor overbright, save for a certain fire in their eyes when they turned them; and they were clothed each from head to foot in an armour of sapphire plates shot with steely emerald. Surely the dragonfly that darteth all day in the blaze over pools is like what they were. Abarak bit his forefinger and said, “Who be ye, O sons of brilliance?”
They answered, “Karavejis and Veejravoosh, slaves of the Sword.”
Then he said, “Come with us now, O slaves of the Sword, and help us to the mountain of outer Aklis.”
They answered, “O thou, there be but two means for us of quitting Aklis: on the wrist of the Master, or down the blade of the Sword! and from the wrist of the Master we have been loosed, and no one of thy race can tie us to it again.”
Abarak said, “How then shall the Master leave Aklis?”
They answered, “By Allah in Aklis! he can carve a way whither he will with the Sword.”
But Abarak cried, “O Karavejis and Veejravoosh! he hath peered through the veil of the Ferrying Figure.”
Now, when they heard his words, the visages of the genii darkened, and they exclaimed sorrowfully, “Serve we such a one?”
And they looked at Shibli Bagarag a look of anger, so that he, whose wits were in past occurrences, imagined them his enemy and the foe of Noorna split in two, crying, “How? Is Karaz a couple? and do I multiply him with strokes of the Sword?”

===Chinese===

可如今，正因他窥探了那道面纱，他只得割舍这两根马鬃才能脱身离开阿克利斯。”阿巴拉克喃喃自语道：“他的命星委实古怪离奇！竟是一颗引着他穿过重重白眼与冷遇迈向亨通、借助万般鞭笞杖责成就伟业的命星！安拉的造化真乃神妙莫测！”希布利·巴加拉格浑然不知反抗，阿巴拉克便从他腕上解下了那两根闪烁的毫毛；只见那两根毫毛迎风胀大，生出磷光闪烁的鳞甲，化作生着浓翠欲滴翡翠羽翼的蓝宝石飞蛇。两条飞蛇彼此缠绕回旋着盘旋升空，一上一下，望之犹如在这无边黑夜中喷涌出一泓闪烁着翡翠光华的蓝宝石泉水。待升至比阿克利斯最高的宫殿塔楼还要高耸的虚空之中，它们如标枪般直插大地，转瞬间重现身形，化作神怪以仁善之心光临凡间时的模样；体型并不比凡人庞大多少，通身亦未耀眼夺目，唯有顾盼之间眼中掠过一缕灼热的烈火；二人自头至脚皆披挂着嵌有钢青翠绿光芒的蓝宝石鳞甲。整日在烈日下的水塘上方疾飞穿梭的蜻蜓，其光彩恰似他们此刻的身姿。阿巴拉克咬着食指问道：“你们是何方神圣，光芒之子啊？”
二神怪应道：“乃是卡拉韦吉斯（Karavejis）与维吉拉武什（Veejravoosh），宝剑的奴仆。”
阿巴拉克于是道：“宝剑的奴仆啊，如今且随我们走吧，助我们登上阿克利斯外围的崇山峻岭。”
他们答道：“你这凡人，我们要想离开阿克利斯唯有两条途径：要么依附在主人的手腕上，要么顺着宝剑的剑刃飞遁！如今既已从主人的手腕上解脱，你所属的凡人之躯便再无人能将我们重新系回腕间。”
阿巴拉克问道：“既然如此，主人该如何脱离阿克利斯？”
他们回答：“凭阿克利斯之境的安拉起誓！他手握宝剑，自可随心所欲劈出一条坦途。”
阿巴拉克却急呼道：“卡拉韦吉斯与维吉拉武什啊！他方才偷看了摆渡蒙面人的面纱之下！”
听到这话，两位神怪的脸色骤然阴沉下来，满含悲戚地惊叹道：“难道我们要侍奉这等蠢物？”
他们怒目瞪向希布利·巴加拉格，而神思仍沉溺于往昔记忆的希布利·巴加拉格，还以为眼前是自己与努尔娜的宿敌一分为二，不禁大声喊道：“怎么？难道卡拉兹（Karaz）变成了一对？莫非我用宝剑劈砍，反倒让他翻了倍不成？”

===Original===

Thereupon he drew the Sword from his girdle in wrath, flourishing it; and Karavejis and Veejravoosh felt the might of the Sword, and prostrated themselves to the ground at his feet. And Abarak said, “Arise, and bring us swiftly to the mountain of outer Aklis.”
Then said they, “Seek a passage down yonder brook in the moonbeams; and it is the sole passage for him now.”
Abarak went with them to the brook that was making watery music to itself between banks of splintered rock and over broad slabs of marble, bubbling here and there about the roots of large-leaved water-flowers, and catching the mirrored moon of Aklis in whirls, breaking it in lances. Then they waded into the water knee-deep, and the two genii seized hold of a great slab of marble in the middle of the water, and under was a hollow brimmed with the brook, that the brook partly filled and flowed over. Then the genii said to Abarak, “Plunge!” and they said the same to Shibli Bagarag. The swayer of the Sword replied, as it had been a simple occasion, a common matter, and a thing for the exercise of civility, “With pleasure and all willingness!” Thereupon he tightened his girth, and arrowing his two hands, flung up his heels and disappeared in the depths, Abarak following. Surely, those two went diving downward till it seemed to each there was no bottom in the depth, and they would not cease to feel the rushing of the water in their ears till the time anticipated by mortals.

===Chinese===

说罢，他怒气冲冲地从腰带间拔出宝剑，在空中一阵挥舞；卡拉韦吉斯与维吉拉武什感受到宝剑的神威，当即匍匐倒地，跪拜在他脚前。阿巴拉克说道：“快快平身，速速领我们前往阿克利斯外围的山峰。”
二神怪答道：“在月华笼罩的那道溪流深处寻一条通路吧；如今这乃是他脱身的唯一途径。”
阿巴拉克随他们来到溪边；那溪水在崩裂嶙峋的岩壁之间、在宽阔平整的大理石板上方自顾自流淌奏鸣，在阔叶水花生长的根须周围咕嘟咕嘟泛起涟漪水泡，在旋涡中倒映出阿克利斯的一轮明月，又将月影撞碎成道道银白长矛。随后他们涉入深及膝弯的水中，两位神怪抓住水流中央的一块巨大大理石板，石板下方露出一处被溪水注满的深潭窟窿，溪水浸漫其间，向外汩汩流淌。两位神怪对阿巴拉克道：“跳下去！”又对希布利·巴加拉格说了同样的话。这位手握宝剑的掌剑之人却像是遇上了一桩寻常小事、一件只需恪守礼数即可的平淡过场，答道：“在下欣然从命，万分乐意！”说罢他扎紧腰带，双手并拢如箭镞向前探出，双足一蹬便没入幽潭深处，阿巴拉克亦紧随其后一跃而入。委实如此，二人径直朝幽深处潜潜沉落，直至在两人感觉中那深渊仿佛永无止境，耳畔水流奔腾咆哮之声直到凡人所预期的时辰也未曾停歇。

## The Well of Paravid / 帕拉维德之井
===Original===

Now, there was haste in the movements of Noorna bin Noorka, and she arrayed herself and clutched Shibli Bagarag by the arm, and the twain departed from Feshnavat the Vizier, and came to the outside of the city, and lo! there was the genie by a well under a palm, and he standing in the shape of an ass, saddled. So they mounted him, and in a moment they were in the midst of the desert, and naught round them save the hot glimmer of the sands and the grey of the sky. Surely, the ass went at such a pace as never ass went before in this world, resting not by the rivulets, nor under the palms, nor beside the date-boughs; it was as if the ass scurried without motion of his legs, so swiftly went he. At last the desert gave signs of a border on the low line of the distance, and this grew rapidly higher as they advanced, revealing a country of hills and rocks, and at the base of these the ass rested.
So Noorna said, “This desert that we have passed, O my betrothed, many are they that perish in it, and reach not the well; but give thanks to Allah that it is passed.”
Then said she, “Dismount, and be wary of moving to the front or to the rear of this ass, and measure thy distance from the lash of his tail.”
So Shibli Bagarag dismounted, and followed her up the hills and the rocks, through ravines and gorges of the rocks, and by tumbling torrents, among hanging woods, over perilous precipices, where no sun hath pierced, and the bones of travellers whiten in loneliness; and they continued mounting upward by winding paths, now closed in by coverts, now upon open heights having great views, and presently a mountain was disclosed to them, green at the sides high up it; and Noorna bin Noorka said to Shibli Bagarag, “Mount here, for the cunning of this ass can furnish him no excuse further for making thee food for the birds of prey.”
So Shibli Bagarag mounted, and they ceased not to ascend the green slopes till the grass became scanty and darkness fell, and they were in a region of snow and cold. Then Noorna bin Noorka tethered the ass to a stump of a tree and breathed in his ear, and the ass became as a creature carved in stone; and she drew from her bosom two bags of silk, and blew in one and entered it, bidding Shibli Bagarag do likewise with the other bag; and he obeyed her, drawing it up to his neck, and the delightfulness of warmth came over him.

===Chinese===

此时，努尔卡之子努尔娜（Noorna bin Noorka）行动甚为急促，她穿戴整齐，一把抓起希布利·巴加拉格（Shibli Bagarag）的手臂，二人辞别宰相费什纳瓦特（Feshnavat），径直来到城外。看哪！只见那神怪正立在一株棕榈树下的井旁，已化作一头备好了鞍鞯的毛驴。二人当即跨上驴背，转瞬间便已置身大漠深处，四顾茫茫，唯见灼热滚烫的茫茫沙浪与灰蒙蒙的苍穹。委实如此，那毛驴奔跑的步伐世间从未有哪头驴能及，遇溪流不歇，逢棕榈不停，过椰枣林亦不作逗留；它疾驰如飞，快得仿佛四蹄未动便已掠过千山万水。最终，极目远眺的地平线上显露出了荒漠的边际，随着二人不断前行，地平线急剧拔高，露出一片丘陵嶙峋、怪石林立的险境，毛驴便在山脚下停住了蹄步。
努尔娜说道：“我订亲的夫君啊，我们方才穿越的这片荒漠，不知有多少人丧命其间、终未抵达灵井；但当感谢安拉，我们已安然度过。”
接着她又嘱咐道：“下来吧，切记不可走到这头毛驴的前头或后头，须得同它抽打的尾巴保持距离。”
希布利·巴加拉格便跳下驴背，尾随在她身后攀登丘陵怪石，穿行于道道石沟峡谷之间，掠过奔腾倾泻的激流，穿过悬岩绝壁上的丛林，越过险象环生的悬崖峭壁——那是日光从未照临之地，路倒旅人的白骨在孤寂中泛着森森白光。他们沿着蜿蜒的小径不断向上攀登，时而被密林遮蔽，时而踏上视野开阔的高岗；不久，一座半山腰郁郁葱葱的高山展现在眼前。努尔卡之子努尔娜对希布利·巴加拉格道：“在这里上驴吧，纵然这头驴再狡黠多诈，此地也再找不出借口将你抛下喂猛禽了。”
希布利·巴加拉格于是重新上驴，他们沿着青翠的山坡不停向上攀行，直至草木渐趋稀疏，暮色四合，二人已置身于一片冰雪严寒之地。努尔卡之子努尔娜将毛驴拴在一截树桩上，对着驴耳轻轻吹了一口气，那驴登时化作一尊石雕般的死物；随后她从怀中取出两只丝袋，对着其中一只吹了口气便钻了进去，同时吩咐希布利·巴加拉格钻进另一只丝袋。青年依言照办，将丝袋一直拉到脖颈处，顿觉一股融融暖意通体舒泰。

===Original===

Then said she, “Tomorrow, at noon, we shall reach near the summit of the mountain and the Well of Paravid, if my power last over this ass; and from that time thou wilt be on the high road to greatness, so fail not to remember what I have done for thee, and be not guilty of ingratitude when thy hand is the stronger.”
He promised her, and they lay and slept. When he awoke the sun was half-risen, and he looked at Noorna bin Noorka in the silken bag, and she was yet in the peacefulness of pleasant dreams; but for the ass, surely his eyes rolled, and his head and fore legs were endued with life, while his latter half seemed of stone. And the youth called to Noorna bin Noorka, and pointed to her the strangeness of the condition of the ass. As she cast eyes on him she cried out, and rushed to him, and took him by the ears and blew up his nostrils, and the animal was quiet. Then she and Shibli Bagarag mounted him again, and she said to him, “It is well thou wert more vigilant than I, and that the sun rose not on this ass while I slept, or my enchantment would have thawed on him, and he would have ’scaped us.”
She gave her heel to the ass, and the ass hung his tail in sullenness and drooped his head; and she laughed, crying, “Karaz, silly fellow! do thy work willingly, and take wisely thine outwitting.”
She jeered him as they journeyed, and made the soul of Shibli Bagarag merry, so that he jerked in his seat upon the ass. Now, as they ascended the mountain they came to the opening of a cavern, and Noorna bin Noorka halted the ass, and said to Shibli Bagarag, “We part here, and I wait for thee in this place. Take this phial, and fill it with the waters of the well, after thy bath. The way is before thee﻿—speed on it.”
He climbed the sides of the mountain, and was soon hidden in the clefts and beyond the perches of the vulture. She kept her eyes on the rocky point when he disappeared, awaiting his return; and the sun went over her head and sank on the yonside of the mountain, and it was by the beams of the moon that she beheld Shibli Bagarag dropping from the crags and ledges of rock, sliding and steadying himself downward till he reached her with the phial in his hand, filled; and he was radiant, as it were divine with freshness, so that Noorna, before she spoke welcome to him, was lost in contemplating the warm shine of his visage, calling to mind the poet’s words:

===Chinese===

随后她说道：“明日正午，只要我的法力还能镇得住这头蠢驴，我们便能抵达山巅附近的帕拉维德之井（Well of Paravid）；自那时起，你便踏上了通往显赫伟业的青云之路。因此切莫忘记我为你所付出的一切，日后当你权势在握、羽翼丰满之时，切不可忘恩负义。”
希布利·巴加拉格向她郑重许诺，二人便躺下沉沉睡去。待他醒来之时，红日已升起半边，他瞧了瞧睡在丝袋中的努尔卡之子努尔娜，见她依旧沉浸在香甜安详的美梦之中；然而那头毛驴却骨碌碌转动着眼珠，脑袋与前蹄已恢复了生机，后半截身子却依然如石头般僵硬。青年急忙唤醒努尔卡之子努尔娜，指着毛驴的怪异模样给她看。她定睛一瞧，惊呼一声，猛扑上前一把揪住驴耳朵，对着它的两个鼻孔吹了一口气，那牲畜这才老实下来。随后她同希布利·巴加拉格重新跨上驴背，她对青年道：“多亏你比我警觉，日光未曾在我熟睡时全然照在这头驴身上，否则施在它身上的法术一旦解冻，它定会从我们手中溜之大吉。”
她用脚跟踢了踢驴肚子，那毛驴怏怏不乐地耷拉着尾巴，垂下脑袋；努尔娜大笑着打趣道：“卡拉兹（Karaz），你这蠢汉！老老实实干活吧，既然斗智落了下风，就乖乖认栽服气吧。”
一路上她不住地嘲弄奚落那驴，逗得希布利·巴加拉格心花怒放，在驴背上笑得前仰后合。当他们攀爬到半山腰时，来到了一处山洞入口，努尔卡之子努尔娜勒停毛驴，对希布利·巴加拉格道：“我们便在此分手，我在此处候你归来。带上这只晶瓶，待你在井中沐浴之后，将瓶中注满井水。前路已在脚下——速去速回吧。”
青年攀上山岩，很快便隐没在崇山峻岭与兀鹰栖息的绝壁深处。努尔娜目不转睛地凝望着他消失的岩角，静候他的归期；烈日自她头顶掠过，缓缓沉入大山背面。直到月洒清辉之际，她才看见希布利·巴加拉格从悬崖峭壁上一级级攀援而下，一路滑降、借力稳住身形，手中握着注满井水的晶瓶回到了她面前；只见他容光焕发，通体透着一股宛若天人的清爽朝气，以至于努尔娜尚未开口道喜，便已痴痴沉醉在他脸上那抹温润动人的光华之中，心中不禁浮现出诗人的诗句：

===Original===

> “The wealth of light in sun and moon,

All nature’s wealth,

Hath mortal beauty for a boon

When match’d with health.”

Then said she, “O Shibli Bagarag, ’tis achieved, this first of thy tasks; for mutely on the fresh red of thy mouth, my betrothed, speaketh the honey of persuasiveness, and the children of Aklis will not resist thee.” So she took the phial from him and led forth the ass, and the twain mounted the ass and descended the slopes of the mountain in moonlight; and Shibli Bagarag said, “Lo! I have marked wonders, and lived a life since our parting; and this well, ’tis a miracle to dip in it, and by it sit many maidens weeping and old men babbling, and youths that were idle youths striking bubbles from the surface of the water. The well is rounded with marble, and the sky is clear in it, cool in it, the whole Earth imaged therein.”
Then Noorna said, “Hadst thou a difficulty in obtaining the waters of the well?”
He answered, “Surely all was made smooth for me by thy aid. Now when I came to the well I marked not them by it, but plunged, and the depth of that well seemed to me the very depth of the Earth itself, so went I ever downward; and when I was near the bottom of the well I had forgotten life above, and lo! no sooner had I touched the bottom of the well when my head emerged from the surface: ’twas wondrous! But for a sign that touched the bottom of the well, see, O Noorna bin Noorka, the Jewel, the one of myriads that glitter at the bottom, and I plucked it for a gift to thee.”
So Noorna took the Jewel from his hand that was torn and crimson, and she cried, “Thou fair youth, thou bleedest with the plucking of it, and it was written, no hand shall pluck a jewel at the bottom of that well without letting of blood. Even so it is! Worthy art thou, and I was not mistaken in thee.”
At her words Shibli Bagarag burst forth into praises of her, and he sang:

> “What is my worthiness

Match’d with thy worth?

Darkness and earthiness,

Dust and dearth!

“O Noorna, thou art wise above women: great and glorious over them.”
In this fashion the youth lauded her that was his betrothed, but she exclaimed, “Hush! or the jealousy of this ass will be aroused, and of a surety he’ll spill us.”
Then he laughed and she laughed till the tail of Karaz trembled.

===Chinese===

> “日月所赋万丈光，
乾坤造化尽琳琅；
凡人之美成至宝，
更借康健耀韶华。”

随后她说道：“希布利·巴加拉格啊，你的首桩重任大功告成；我订亲的夫君，如今在你鲜红饱满的双唇之上，默默流淌着令人折服的甘美口才，阿克利斯之子（children of Aklis）绝难抵御你的言辞。”说罢，她从青年手中接过晶瓶，牵过毛驴，二人跨上驴背，在皎洁的月色中顺着山坡向下行去。希布利·巴加拉格叹道：“看哪！自我们分手以来，我饱览奇观，仿佛重活了一世；那口井，跳入其中当真神妙无双——井旁坐着许多垂泪的少女和絮絮叨叨的老叟，还有游手好闲的青年正拍打着水面上的水泡玩耍。整座水井以大理石环砌而成，苍穹倒映其间，清澄明彻、清凉怡人，仿佛整个人间大地皆尽收其中。”
努尔娜问道：“你取这井水可曾历经艰险？”
他答道：“全仗你的庇佑，一切皆顺遂无比。方才我来到井边，并未多看井旁众人一眼，纵身便跃入水中；只觉那井水幽深宛如直通大地最深处，我便这般一直向下潜去；待接近井底时，我早已将尘世万物忘得一干二净。看哪！正当我触及井底的刹那，我的脑袋竟奇迹般浮出了水面——当真是不可思议！至于我曾潜及井底的明证，看哪，努尔卡之子努尔娜，这颗宝珠（Jewel）乃是井底千万颗闪烁宝珠中的一颗，我特意将它采来赠予你。”
努尔娜从他那擦伤流血、通红的手掌中接过宝珠，惊呼道：“好一位俊朗青年，你为采这宝珠竟伤痕累累、鲜血淋漓；古书有云，若非流血挂彩，绝无凡人之手能采得井底宝珠。果不其然！你当真配得上这番作为，我果然没有看错你。”
听闻此言，希布利·巴加拉格情不自禁对她大加赞美，放声高歌道：

> “若比卿之尊与德，
我身何足论轩昂？
不过幽暗凡俗质，
满目尘埃尽枯黄！

“噢，努尔娜，你的智慧凌驾于众妇之上，尊荣显赫远胜群芳。”
青年便是这般赞美自己的未婚妻，努尔娜却急忙喊道：“嘘！快住嘴！否则这头驴的妒火被激起来，准会把我们俩全掀翻在地不可。”
听到这话，他不由得哈哈大笑，努尔娜也跟着笑了起来，直笑得卡拉兹的驴尾巴都跟着颤抖不已。

## The Wiles of Rabesqurat / 拉贝斯库拉特的诡计
===Original===

Now, when Noorna bin Noorka had made an end of her narration, she folded her hands and was mute awhile; and to the ear of Shibli Bagarag it seemed as if a sweet instrument had on a sudden ceased luting. So, as he leaned, listening for her voice to recommence, she said quickly, “See yonder fire on the mountain’s height!”
He looked and saw a great light on the summit of a lofty mountain before them.
Then said she, “That is Aklis! and it is ablaze, knowing a visitant near. Tighten now the hairs of Garraveen about thy wrist; touch thy lips with the waters of Paravid; hold before thee the Lily, and make ready to enter the mountain. Lo, my betrothed, thou art in possession of the three means that melt opposition, and the fault is thine if thou fail.”
He did as she directed; and they were taken on a tide and advanced rapidly to the mountain, so that the waters smacked and crackled beneath the shell, covering it with silver showering arches of glittering spray. Then the fair beams of the moon became obscured, and the twain reddened with the reflection of the fire, and the billows waxed like riotous flames; and presently the shell rose upon the peak of many waves swollen to one, and looking below, they saw in the scarlet abyss of waters at their feet a monstrous fish, with open jaws and one baleful eye; and the fish was lengthy as a caravan winding through the desert, and covered with fiery scales. Shibli Bagarag heard the voice of Noorna shriek affrightedly, “Karaz!” and as they were sliding on the down slope, she stood upright in the shell, pronouncing rapidly some words in magic; and the shell closed upon them both, pressing them together, and writing darkness on their very eyeballs. So, while they were thus, they felt themselves gulped in, and borne forward with terrible swiftness, they knew not where, like one that hath a dream of sinking; and outside the shell a rushing, gurgling noise, and a noise as of shouting multitudes, and muffled multitudes muttering complaints and yells and querulous cries, told them they were yet speeding through the body of the depths in the belly of the fish. Then there came a shock, and the shell was struck with light, and they were sensible of stillness without motion.

===Chinese===

此时，当努尔卡之子努尔娜（Noorna bin Noorka）讲完故事后，她双手交叠，静默了片刻；在希布利·巴加拉格（Shibli Bagarag）听来，这沉寂宛如一架甜美动人的乐器骤然停歇了弹奏。正当他侧耳倾听、盼着她的声音再度响起时，她忽然急促地说道：“瞧那山巅上的烈焰！”
他抬眼望去，只见前方一座巍峨险峰的顶端正闪烁着一片耀眼的火光。
努尔娜接着说道：“那便是阿克利斯（Aklis）！它察觉到有访客临近，故而烈焰腾空。现在快将加拉文（Garraveen）的马鬃在手腕上系紧；用帕拉维德（Paravid）的灵泉润泽双唇；将百合花横在身前，准备进入山中。看哪，我订亲的夫君，消除阻碍的三件至宝皆已为你所有，倘若失败，错便全在你自己了。”
他依言照办；二人乘着汹涌的潮水疾速朝大山逼近，水浪拍打在巨贝下噼啪作响，泼洒下一道道银白晶莹的飞沫水拱，将贝壳笼罩其中。随后皎洁的月色渐趋隐没，二人被那漫天火光映得通红，波涛恰似狂乱的烈焰翻腾起伏；不消片刻，巨贝被多股交汇隆起的巨浪托举至浪尖。二人俯瞰脚下，只见赤红的水渊深处横卧着一头巨怪大鱼，巨口大张，生着一只凶光毕露的独眼；那大鱼长如蜿蜒穿过荒漠的驼队，通身覆满了烈火般的红鳞。希布利·巴加拉格听见努尔娜惊恐万分地尖叫一声：“卡拉兹（Karaz）！”正当贝壳顺着浪坡向下疾滑之际，她在贝壳中霍然挺立，口中疾速念动咒语；巨贝当即合拢，将二人紧紧挤压在一起，令眼前陷入一片漆黑。置身其中的二人只觉被巨怪一口吞下，以骇人的速度不知向前飞驰向何方，恍若凡人在梦魇中直坠深渊；贝壳外水流奔腾汹涌的汩汩声，以及宛如万众高呼呐喊、无数沉闷的声音嘈杂嘟囔、狂号与哀鸣，昭示着他们正穿过深邃无底的水渊，在鱼腹之中飞速疾驰。随后传来一声震天剧震，贝壳被一道亮光击中，他们顿觉天地止息，周遭一片死寂。

===Original===

Then a blow on the shell shivered it to fragments, and they were blinded with seas of brilliancy on all sides from lamps and tapers and crystals, cornelians and gems of fiery lustre, liquid lights and flashing mirrors, and eyes of crowding damsels, bright ones.
